Trial budget preserved at 360 trials total (≥30 per staircase × 12 staircases). **The methodology shift closes qa-lead F5's "p95(t_decision)≤0.15s incompatible with REACTION_BUDGET=0.20s" finding**: prior RT paradigm conflated perceptibility-onset latency AND motor reaction time (mathematically impossible to satisfy when motor RT alone is 200ms); 2-AFC decouples these — the resulting `t_threshold` is pure perceptibility latency, directly comparable to `t_perceptible_direction` in EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY. Evidence path updated from `t_decision histograms` to per-staircase trajectories + last-6-reversal threshold estimates + 3-tester median per tier. The 2-AFC paradigm is psychophysics gold standard for perceptibility-floor measurement; the prior RT paradigm was a measurement-design defect, not just a parameter mis-choice. ## Overview

Pull-Wave Behavior is the magnetized hazard the player reads, leans away from, and slips. Each pull-wave is a single voxel mass that spawns at an authored lane on the far edge of the track, leans toward a target lane during its telegraph window, then surges along a designer-authored trajectory curve toward the player's plane. Multiple pull-waves can be in flight simultaneously; their density, telegraph window, and concurrency are bound by the Difficulty & Phase Controller, but their behavior — how they reach, how they lean, how they land — is owned entirely by this system. Pull-Wave is the only hazard in SLIPSTORM; without it there is nothing for slip to resolve. The player never names a pull-wave; they name the moment it reached for them and the moment they were not where it landed. Pull-Wave's data-driven authoring (per-pattern scripted curves, no runtime physics) keeps the magnetism a designed illusion — the world *reaches*, but it reaches on rails the designer drew. Track lane count is bound at **=5** by this GDD (R1 RC-F adjudication — see Dependencies) to satisfy DPC's AC-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K spatial-variety requirement with margin (C(5,3)=10 ≥ 4) while preserving thumb-target floor at 6-inch mobile screen; this raises Player Movement's prior 3-lane track and forces a downstream Player Movement revision.

## Player Fantasy

Before a pull-wave moves, it tilts. A half-beat of lean — barely a frame longer than instinct — and you know which way the iron is about to come for you. The pull-wave's whole identity lives in that tilt: it is a hazard that *tells* on itself, a predator that draws breath before the lunge. You read the lean, you feel the commit, you are already bending the other way. The iron doesn't *fall* toward you the way a real magnet would — it *arrives*, on a curve someone drew, at a speed someone tuned. That's the contract. The world telegraphs because the world is honest, and the curve is scripted because honesty needs a steady hand.

The pull-wave is not a projectile and not a swing. It is a posture. Its mass commits to a destination lane during the lean, its trajectory carries it home, and it lands where the lean said it would land — never anywhere else. A player who reads the lean sees the iron pass through the space they just left, on the path the lean drew, at the speed the lean implied. A player who misreads dies attributable, with the lean still visible in memory and the trajectory still visible on the track. The pull-wave never lies. It cannot lie — its curve is published before its motion begins.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

1. **Domain boundary.** Pull-Wave owns each wave instance's lifecycle — creation, lean behavior, trajectory evaluation, landing resolution, despawn. It does NOT own spawn cadence (Wave Spawner), telegraph *rendering* (Telegraph System — Pull-Wave owns the lean *behavior*, Telegraph owns the visual presentation), or audio responses (Audio Controller). Pull-Wave calls `PM.TriggerNearMissBeat()` directly; all other downstream effects fire via events Pull-Wave publishes (`OnWaveHit`, `OnNearMiss`, `OnWaveDespawned`).

2. **Five-lane track (BINDING — forces PM revision).** The track has exactly **5 lanes** (indices 0–4, left to right). At `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1` (**target-lane distinct** — R2 Cluster E CD adjudication: separation measured on target lanes, not source lanes; adjacency permitted), this yields C(5,3) = **10 valid M=3 spatial configurations** for barrage patterns — satisfies DPC R7's AC-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K ≥4 requirement with margin. **Lane count selected over 6-lane alternative (R1 CD adjudication 2026-06-07)** because at LANE_WIDTH_M = 1.0 m, on-screen lane width on a 6-inch mobile screen sits comfortably above the thumb-target floor (≈14 mm vs ≈11.7 mm at 6 lanes); C(5,3)=10 still provides 2.5× the AC ≥4 floor. Supersedes PM Rule 1's 3-lane model; forces revision of `EPlayerLane` enum and `LANE_OFFSET_CM` mapping via `/propagate-design-change` post-this GDD's completion. Reducing lane count below 5 requires re-verifying the AC against the then-current `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION`; raising above 5 reopens the mobile-thumb-target check.

3. **Five-state lifecycle.** Each wave progresses through exactly five states, with no backward transitions:

   | State | Purpose |
   |---|---|
   | `SPAWNED` | One-tick init: targeting committed, Collision + Telegraph registered. Not yet visible to lean readers. |
   | `LEANING` | Forward-stationary; voxel mass tilts toward `target_lane` over `lean_duration_s`. Telegraph reads `lean_progress` + `target_lane`. |
   | `TRAVERSING` | Forward motion + lateral curve evaluation. Collision reads world position per tick. |
   | `LANDED` | Wave reached player plane; collision check fired at entry, `collision_outcome` field set at entry. State persists for `WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S` (default `0.15s` ≈ 9 ticks at 60 fps) to let Telegraph's landing-impact VFX complete before DESPAWNING. No re-fires of OnWaveHit / OnNearMiss during the hold (Rule 13, AC-PW-14). |
   | `DESPAWNING` | Cleanup pipeline: Collision unregister → Telegraph unregister → broadcast `OnWaveDespawned` → mesh hide → SM state cleared → return to pool. |

   Forced despawn (RSM exits RUNNING) skips LANDED hit/near-miss resolution and routes directly to DESPAWNING; no hit/near-miss event published. See "States and Transitions" sub-section for the full transition table and pause-flush semantics.

4. **Target lane locked at SPAWNED.** `target_lane` (`int32 ∈ [0, 4]`) is assigned at Wave Spawner's `Construct(...)` call and is `const` for the wave's lifetime. The lean animation publishes a decision already made — never a decision in progress. No system may mutate `target_lane` after SPAWNED entry. `LateralOffsetCurve` (captured as an immutable snapshot per Rule 14), `forward_velocity_ms`, `source_lane`, `wave_id`, and `lean_duration_s` are likewise frozen at spawn.

5. **Lean is forward-stationary.** During LEANING, the wave's forward world-Z position does NOT advance. The state lasts exactly `lean_duration_s` seconds, read once from `DPC.GetCurrentFrameState().telegraph_window_s` at SPAWNED entry and floored at `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S = 0.70s` (R10d binding — raised 0.65 → 0.70s 2026-06-17 per CD ruling path (i); propagates to DPC GDD + registry post-R11-APPROVED; historical: raised R2 Cluster E path (b) from prior 0.6s; see entity registry and Tuning Knobs). Pull-Wave does NOT re-read the DPC snapshot mid-lean — lean duration is deterministic for the wave's life regardless of DPC phase changes during the lean. LEANING→TRAVERSING fires when `lean_progress >= 1.0` with no gap frame.

6. **Spawn-time velocity binding (DPC R7 forward contract).** `forward_velocity_ms` is set at SPAWNED entry from the per-pattern asset and is **never updated mid-flight**. A wave that spawns during OPENER retains its OPENER-authored velocity even if RSM's run clock crosses MID or PEAK during TRAVERSING. Closes OQ-DPC-2 as **NO phase-driven velocity scaling**; enforces Pillar 5 (no wave mysteriously accelerates after telegraph).

7. **Phase-independent behavior.** Pull-Wave does NOT read `DPC.current_phase`, `wave_spawn_interval_s`, or any DPC scalar at tick time. All wave parameters are authored in the per-pattern asset and frozen at SPAWNED. Difficulty arc is the product of Wave Spawner's cadence + DPC's `telegraph_window_s` narrowing. Individual wave behavior is constant across the run arc.

8. **Trajectory evaluation (see F-TRAJ-TNORM, F-TRAJ-LATERAL, F-TRAJ-FORWARD).** During TRAVERSING, world position each frame is a pure function of `t_norm = clamp(TraverseElapsedS / TravelDurationS, 0.0, 1.0)` (F-TRAJ-TNORM): `world_x` = source-lane X + `CurveSnapshot.EvaluateAt(t_norm) × (target_lane − source_lane) × LANE_WIDTH_M` (F-TRAJ-LATERAL); `world_z` = spawn-plane Z − `t_norm × SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` (F-TRAJ-FORWARD). `TraverseElapsedS` is the canonical time source — no formula reads `GameTime` after SPAWNED bookkeeping (R2 Cluster A fix); pause duration is structurally excluded from the accumulator and cannot teleport the wave on resume (Pillar 5). Forward motion is linear; lateral motion is curve-driven via the per-instance immutable snapshot (Rule 14 (a)). Both evaluated on game thread after PM's tick.

9. **Landing trigger (threshold-cross).** TRAVERSING→LANDED fires on the first tick where `wave.world_z ≤ player_plane_z` (threshold-cross, not equality — protects against frame-hitch overshoot). `t_norm` reaching 1.0 does NOT trigger landing if `world_z > player_plane_z` (authoring error: forward velocity too low for the track depth — cook-time tooling flags this).

10. **Hit resolution.** At LANDED entry, Pull-Wave reads `PM.movement_state` and `PM.current_lane`/`PM.target_lane`. A **hit** is declared when either:
    - `PM.movement_state == SETTLED` AND `PM.current_lane == wave.target_lane`, OR
    - `PM.movement_state == SLIPPING` AND `PM.target_lane == wave.target_lane` (player committed *into* the wave's destination).

    On hit: publish `OnWaveHit(wave_id, target_lane, source_lane, spawn_time)`. Collision GDD subscribes and is responsible for the RSM→DEAD transition; Pull-Wave does NOT call RSM. Pull-Wave proceeds to DESPAWNING after the LANDED hold without waiting for the RSM transition.

11. **Near-miss resolution (direct read of `PM.current_lane` — R1 CD adjudication 2026-06-07).** At LANDED entry, Pull-Wave reads `PM.movement_state` and `PM.current_lane`. A **near-miss** is declared when:
    - `PM.movement_state == SLIPPING` AND `PM.current_lane == wave.target_lane` (the wave is landing in the lane the player is in the act of leaving — *"I read it, and they were already gone."*)

    This works because **PM Rules 4 + 7 guarantee `PM.current_lane` returns the SOURCE lane throughout the SLIPPING state** — `current_lane` is reassigned to `target_lane` only at SLIPPING → SETTLED completion. The wave therefore reads the player's pre-slip lane by reading `current_lane` directly at LANDED — no event subscription, no midpoint timing, no fallback inference required.

    On near-miss: call `PM.TriggerNearMissBeat()` AND publish `OnNearMiss(wave_id, target_lane, slipped_from_lane = PM.current_lane)` for Audio Controller + Camera + VFX subscribers. The `slipped_from_lane` field in the broadcast carries the value read from `PM.current_lane` at LANDED.

    No subscription to `PM.OnSlipMidpoint` is required by Pull-Wave (R1 supersedes R1-draft's recording mechanism). PM may retain or remove `OnSlipMidpoint` independently — Pull-Wave does not consume it.

12. **No cross-wave physics.** Multiple TRAVERSING waves are fully independent — no spatial exclusion, no inter-wave collision, no trajectory modification. Two waves may occupy the same world-space voxel simultaneously with no runtime consequence. The only multi-wave spatial constraint is `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION` (cook-time enforced by Wave Spawner). Pull-Wave's per-instance state is self-contained; the system maintains an active-wave list for (a) per-tick state advancement (iterated in `wave_id ASC` per Rule 14 (b)) and (b) despawn bookkeeping — no longer for `OnSlipMidpoint` routing (R1 RC-A removed that subscription).

13. **Despawn pipeline (ordered, atomic per wave; R4 RC-R3-4 — Telegraph step independently observable).** DESPAWNING entry executes in this exact order in the same tick:
    1. Unregister from Collision: the despawn driver calls `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnCollisionUnregistered(wave_id)` (R1 RC-E E-2 fix). Production's `FWaveSpawnerCallback_Production::OnCollisionUnregistered` forwards to `Collision->UnregisterWave(wave_id)` synchronously **in the same tick** (R4 forward contract on Collision — see Dependencies; deferred-to-next-tick `UnregisterWave` is forbidden). The test stub's `FWaveSpawnerCallbackTestStub::OnCollisionUnregistered` records the invocation so `WasCollisionUnregisteredBeforeBroadcast()` (Seam 13) can validate the ordering invariant against step 3.
    2. Unregister from Telegraph System: the despawn driver calls `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnTelegraphUnregistered(wave_id)` (R4 RC-R3-4 — NEW Seam 13 extension). Production's `FWaveSpawnerCallback_Production::OnTelegraphUnregistered` forwards to `Telegraph->UnregisterWave(wave_id)` synchronously. The test stub records the invocation so AC-PW-15 can assert step (ii) occurred BETWEEN step (i) (`OnCollisionUnregistered`) and step (iii) (`OnWaveDespawned`) using the unified `GetEventLogInOrder()` event log. Without this seam extension, step (ii) was unobservable by the test harness — a reversed-(ii)/(iii) implementation would pass all assertions because the stub only saw (i) and (iii) (R3 RC-R3-4 finding).
    3. Broadcast `OnWaveDespawned(wave_id, despawn_reason, final_lane)` via `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnWaveDespawned`.
    4. Hide mesh / submit to despawn VFX.
    5. Clear per-instance SM state.
    6. Return to Wave Spawner's object pool (or free).

    Steps 5–6 may defer one frame if despawn VFX holds a reference; steps 1–3 MUST complete synchronously in the DESPAWNING-entry tick. Step 1 is a single call: `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnCollisionUnregistered(wave_id)`. Step 2 is a single call: `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnTelegraphUnregistered(wave_id)`. The production classes internally forward to `Collision->UnregisterWave` / `Telegraph->UnregisterWave` synchronously before returning; there is exactly one implementation pattern per call (pattern β — seam-as-unregister). The "two valid implementations" framing is retired (R2 CD adjudication — optionality in a contract is a defect).

14. **Determinism.** A wave's position at any moment is a pure function of `(source_lane, target_lane, curve_snapshot, forward_velocity_ms, lean_duration_s, spawn_time_s, elapsed_time_s)`. No RNG, no physics simulation, no per-frame jitter, no shared mutable state writes (except event broadcasts). Identical inputs produce bit-identical trajectories. Two specific guarantees back this claim (R1 RC-C C-3 + C-4 fixes):

    **(a) Curve snapshot at SPAWNED entry (R1 RC-C C-3).** `LateralOffsetCurve` is NOT stored as a live `TObjectPtr<UCurveFloat>` reference on the wave instance. Instead, at SPAWNED entry, the curve is **sampled into an immutable per-instance `FPullWaveCurveSnapshot`** (see Data Structures sub-section) that is `const` for the wave's lifetime. Subsequent edits to the source `UCurveFloat` asset (hot-reload, editor rebake, etc.) DO NOT affect any in-flight wave. Death Replay captures `FPullWaveSpawnParams` including the snapshot bytes (or a stable curve-asset identifier + sampled-snapshot fingerprint) — the asset reference itself is never load-bearing for replay.

    **(b) Active-wave iteration order — wave_id ASCENDING (R1 RC-C C-4; R4 RC-R3-4 container binding; R4 editorial — type unified to `int32`).** Each tick, Pull-Wave iterates its active-wave list in **strictly ascending `wave_id` order**. `wave_id` is a monotonically increasing **`int32`** (R4 editorial — unified from the R1-draft `uint32` per the R3 editorial defect; matches UE5 delegate-parameter idiom + Seam 13 interface signatures + test stub fields. Wrap-around safety: at peak 1.0 spawn/s sustained, `int32` overflow at ~68 years of continuous play — structurally not reachable in a 60-second run game) assigned at Wave Spawner admission time (one per `Construct()` call). The iteration order governs (i) per-wave tick advancement, (ii) `OnWaveHit` / `OnNearMiss` / `OnWaveDespawned` broadcast order within a tick, and (iii) any cross-wave bookkeeping reads. This makes the bit-identical claim survive across runs with identical spawn sequences regardless of pool-allocator layout or platform ABI. Wave Spawner MUST allocate `wave_id` monotonically and deterministically from a documented initial allocator state; recycled pool slots receive a NEW `wave_id`, never the despawned wave's prior id.

    **Container-type binding (R4 RC-R3-4; R6 B7 — ADD idiom binding extension; R7 B1 — TSortedMap escape-hatch removed against AC-PW-22b pattern 9 ALLOW grep mismatch).** The active-wave list MUST be a `TArray<FPullWaveInstanceState>` with **order-preserving removal** AND **append-only insertion** — i.e., `TArray::RemoveAt(Index)` for removal (NOT `RemoveAtSwap`); `TArray::Add(NewState)` for insertion (NOT `TArray::Insert(NewState, Index)` at any non-tail position). R6 B7 closes the insertion-side gap: because Wave Spawner allocates `wave_id` monotonically AND the active-wave list appends at construction, the `wave_id ASC` invariant is maintained at insertion without any explicit sort. A non-append idiom (`Insert` at index 0, pool-slot-determined index, etc.) would silently break the invariant for same-tick multi-admission cases; AC-PW-10's R6 GIVEN extension exercises same-tick multi-admission and AC-PW-22b pattern 11 (FORBID `TArray::Insert` on active-wave list source) is the design-layer static guard. The R4 binding (`RemoveAtSwap`-style swap-remove idioms are NOT permitted) stands unchanged. `RemoveAtSwap` silently breaks the `wave_id ASC` invariant after the first natural despawn: it overwrites the removed slot with the array's last element, which has a higher `wave_id`. A `TArray<...>` with `RemoveAt` is O(N) per removal; at `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP = 16`, the per-tick overhead is structurally negligible (≤16 element shifts on a 188-byte struct). **R7 B1 binding (no alternative containers permitted)**: prior text named `TSortedMap<int32, FPullWaveInstanceState>` as an "alternative compliant container"; this clause is RETIRED because `TSortedMap` exposes `Remove(Key)` not `RemoveAt(Index)` and would fail AC-PW-22b pattern 9 ALLOW grep `RemoveAt\b` on a conformant implementation — internal contradiction. `TArray` is now the sole permitted container; the O(N) cost at N≤16 is structurally negligible, and a single container choice makes the AC-PW-22b grep patterns unambiguous. CI grep AC-PW-22b pattern (9) FORBIDs `RemoveAtSwap` and ALLOWs `RemoveAt` in the active-wave-list source path. Without this binding, the bit-identical determinism guarantee silently breaks after the first mid-window despawn — AC-PW-10's 100-tick GIVEN does NOT exercise mid-window despawn and would pass; AC-PW-28's two-same-tick-landing assertion would also pass (both waves alive simultaneously). The container-type binding closes this implementation-determined hole at design layer.

    Death Replay reconstructs trajectories from captured `FPullWaveSpawnParams` per Rule 14; no per-frame state capture required.

### States and Transitions

| State | Entry Trigger | Observable Snapshot Fields | Exit Trigger(s) | Exit → |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SPAWNED` | Wave Spawner `Construct()` | `wave_id`, `source_lane`, `target_lane` | Next tick (unconditional) | LEANING |
| `LEANING` | SPAWNED exit | `state`, `target_lane`, `lean_progress` ∈ [0.0, 1.0] | `lean_progress >= 1.0` OR pause-flush OR run-termination | TRAVERSING / DESPAWNING |
| `TRAVERSING` | LEANING exit | `state`, `target_lane`, `lateral_world_position`, `forward_position` | `world_z <= player_plane_z` (threshold-cross) OR pause-flush OR run-termination | LANDED / DESPAWNING |
| `LANDED` | TRAVERSING exit | `state`, `target_lane`, `collision_outcome` ∈ {Hit, NearMiss, CleanMiss} | End of `WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S` hold OR pause-flush | DESPAWNING |
| `DESPAWNING` | Any natural or forced exit | `state`, `despawn_reason` ∈ {NaturalLanding, RunTermination, PauseFlush} | Cleanup complete (1 tick) | (destroyed / pooled) |

**Pause-freeze.** When RSM `is_paused == true`, all in-flight waves freeze: `lean_progress` and `traverse_elapsed_s` accumulators stop advancing (gate: `if RSM.GetIsPaused() return;` at the top of Pull-Wave's tick). State enum unchanged; allocations preserved. Visual stays live (frozen mesh), Collision registration stays live (no spurious events fire because forward position doesn't advance).

**Pause-resume flush (R7 B13 — explicit queued-to-next-tick semantic added; closes mid-tick delegate fire ambiguity).** On `RSM.OnPausedChanged(is_paused=false)` from RUNNING (NOT from ABORTED — see below), Wave Spawner flushes all in-flight waves per RSM Rule 19: every wave in LEANING / TRAVERSING / LANDED transitions to DESPAWNING with `despawn_reason = PauseFlush`. **R7 B13 BINDING — pause-flush is QUEUED for the NEXT TICK start, NOT applied inline mid-tick**: when `RSM.OnPausedChanged(false)` fires from any source (RSM's own tick, an external game-mode manager, a Wave Spawner-side callback, etc.), Pull-Wave does NOT immediately route active waves to DESPAWNING inside the delegate handler. Instead, Pull-Wave sets a single boolean flag `bPauseFlushPending = true` synchronously in the delegate handler; the flag is consumed at the TOP of Pull-Wave's next tick (BEFORE the per-wave iteration loop begins), at which point every active wave in LEANING / TRAVERSING / LANDED is routed to DESPAWNING with `despawn_reason = PauseFlush` AS A BATCH within that single tick. Rationale: an inline mid-tick flush (waves N..N+k routed to DESPAWNING while waves N+k+1..15 are still mid-iteration in the same frame) produces inconsistent per-wave state for that frame — waves 0..N would have had their per-wave "paused, skip" tick treatment already applied, waves N+1..15 would not yet have been ticked, and the flush mid-iteration would interleave DESPAWNING-entry seam events with the in-progress tick's other event broadcasts in a way that violates AC-PW-15's contiguity assertion. The next-tick batch flush makes the flush atomic-as-a-batch: all flushed waves execute Rule 13's despawn pipeline in `wave_id ASC` order within the flush tick, contiguity per wave_id is preserved, and the AC-PW-15 R7 B4 multi-wave-same-tick GIVEN exercises this exact scenario. Trajectory began before background event; returning player has no readable telegraph context (Pillar 2 violation otherwise). New spawns resume only after `resume_grace` clears. See AC-PW-MID-TICK-PAUSE-DEFERRAL for the assertion that mid-tick `OnPausedChanged(false)` fire is deferred to next tick start.

**Stale-pause ABORTED.** RSM transitions RUNNING(paused) → ABORTED: `is_paused` becomes false but Rule 19 flush does NOT fire (ABORTED is not RUNNING). Frozen waves unfreeze (accumulators resume), drain to natural end via TRAVERSING → LANDED → DESPAWNING with `despawn_reason = RunTermination`. Distinct from PauseFlush.

**Run-termination (DEAD / COMPLETE).** RSM transitions RUNNING → DEAD/COMPLETE/ABORTED mid-flight. DPC publishes `is_active = false` halting new spawns. In-flight waves continue advancing through TRAVERSING → LANDED → DESPAWNING with `despawn_reason = RunTermination`. Per DPC run-termination drain contract + Collision GDD post-RUNNING hit policy (R6 CD-bound Option B): LANDED collision events still fire and are bookkept against the just-ended run, but cannot flip the terminal RSM state. Pull-Wave fires the event; Collision GDD owns the gameplay effect.

### Interactions with Other Systems

**Upstream — Wave Spawner (downstream-of-DPC, upstream-of-Pull-Wave).** Wave Spawner calls Pull-Wave's `Construct(FPullWaveSpawnParams)` to instantiate each wave. The params struct:
```cpp
struct FPullWaveSpawnParams {
    int32  WaveId;                       // monotonically increasing per Construct() call; never reused (Rule 14 ordering invariant). R4 editorial: type unified to int32 to match Seam 13 + delegate idiom.
    int32  SourceLane;                   // [0, 4]
    int32  TargetLane;                   // [0, 4]
    FPullWaveCurveSnapshot CurveSnapshot; // immutable sampled copy of LateralOffsetCurve (Rule 14 (a))
    float  ForwardVelocityMs;            // m/s, ∈ [PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MIN_MS, PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MAX_MS]
    float  SpawnTimeS;                   // absolute game-time at spawn
    float  LeanDurationS;                // = clamp(DPC.telegraph_window_s, TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S, ∞) — captured by Wave Spawner from DPC snapshot at admit time
};
```
Wave Spawner validates the source `UCurveFloat` asset at admission time (asserts in entity registry's Tuning Knobs section), produces the `FPullWaveCurveSnapshot` (Rule 14 (a)), then passes the snapshot — not the asset reference — into `Construct()`. Asset-side endpoint validation: `LateralOffsetCurve.GetFloatValue(0.0) == 0.0` AND `|GetFloatValue(1.0) − 1.0| < CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE = 0.005`. Wave Spawner's `max_concurrent_waves` cap (DPC-bound, ≤16) is enforced upstream; Pull-Wave does not self-limit. Pull-Wave trusts the cap.

**Data Structures — `FPullWaveCurveSnapshot` + `FPullWaveInstanceState` (R1 RC-C + RC-E fixes).** Two supporting per-instance structs:

```cpp
// Immutable, value-typed snapshot of LateralOffsetCurve (Rule 14 (a)).
// Sampled by Wave Spawner at admission time, copied by value into FPullWaveSpawnParams.
// Sample count chosen so linear interpolation between samples does not exceed
// CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE at any interior t_norm under expected curve smoothness.
struct FPullWaveCurveSnapshot {
    static constexpr int32 SAMPLE_COUNT = 32;          // locked; power of 2; step = 1/31 ≈ 0.032 t_norm
                                                       // Interpolation error ≤ 0.001 at SAMPLE_COUNT=32
                                                       // (5× under CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE=0.005; see
                                                       // byte-budget note below). SAMPLE_COUNT=16 is NOT
                                                       // a valid tuning option — error ≈ 0.003 (66% of
                                                       // tolerance, unsafe margin).
    float                  Samples[SAMPLE_COUNT];      // sampled at t_norm = i / (SAMPLE_COUNT - 1)
    // Linear interpolation at runtime — pure function, no asset reference held.
    // EvaluateAt clamps: i0 = (int)(TNormClamped * (SAMPLE_COUNT-1)); i1 = min(i0+1, SAMPLE_COUNT-1).
    // EvaluateAt(1.0) returns Samples[SAMPLE_COUNT-1] — NOT Samples[SAMPLE_COUNT] (OOB prevented by i1 clamp).
    float EvaluateAt(float TNormClamped) const;        // inline; TNormClamped pre-clamped to [0,1] by caller
};

// Per-instance runtime state. Cache-friendly size budget (AC-PW-22b): ≤ 256 bytes.
struct FPullWaveInstanceState {
    int32                  WaveId;                     // assigned at Construct(); Rule 14 (b) iteration key. R4 editorial: type unified to int32.
    int32                  SourceLane;                 // [0, 4]; const after SPAWNED
    int32                  TargetLane;                 // [0, 4]; const after SPAWNED
    EPullWaveState         State;                      // SPAWNED / LEANING / TRAVERSING / LANDED / DESPAWNING
    float                  SpawnTimeS;                 // const after SPAWNED
    float                  LeanDurationS;              // const after SPAWNED
    float                  ForwardVelocityMs;          // const after SPAWNED
    float                  TravelDurationS;            // derived from F-TRAVERSE-DURATION at SPAWNED
    float                  LeanProgress;               // Accumulator for lean phase. Initialized to 0.0 at LEANING entry.
                                                       // Advances by (DeltaTime / LeanDurationS) each LEANING tick where
                                                       // RSM.GetIsPaused() == false. Frozen across pause boundaries —
                                                       // pause duration NOT included (R2 Cluster A symmetry with TraverseElapsedS).
                                                       // Cleared to 0.0 at LEANING exit. Range: [0.0, 1.0] during LEANING; 0.0 elsewhere.
    float                  TraverseElapsedS;           // Canonical time-source for F-TRAJ-TNORM (R2 Cluster A fix).
                                                       // Initialized to 0.0 at TRAVERSING entry. Advances by DeltaTime each
                                                       // TRAVERSING tick where RSM.GetIsPaused() == false. Frozen across
                                                       // pause boundaries — pause duration NOT included. At LANDED entry:
                                                       // clamped to TravelDurationS (handles frame-hitch overshoot where a
                                                       // long DeltaTime tick pushed TraverseElapsedS past TravelDurationS),
                                                       // then held at TravelDurationS throughout WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S so
                                                       // t_norm == 1.0 is maintained. Range: [0.0, TravelDurationS].
    float                  LandedHoldElapsedS;         // [0, WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S] during LANDED
    float                  WorldX;                     // last-tick output of F-TRAJ-LATERAL
    float                  WorldZ;                     // last-tick output of F-TRAJ-FORWARD
    ECollisionOutcome      CollisionOutcome;           // Hit / NearMiss / CleanMiss; set at LANDED entry, then const
    EDespawnReason         DespawnReason;              // NaturalLanding / RunTermination / PauseFlush; set at DESPAWNING entry
    FPullWaveCurveSnapshot CurveSnapshot;              // immutable per Rule 14 (a)
    // Total size: 15 × 4 = 60 bytes scalar + 128 bytes Samples (SAMPLE_COUNT=32) = 188 bytes.
    // 188 ≤ 256 — satisfies AC-PW-22b static_assert with 68 bytes headroom. LOCKED; not a prototype knob.
    // Active-wave list is iterated wave_id ASCENDING per Rule 14 (b).
};
```

`AC-PW-22b` greps for `static_assert(sizeof(FPullWaveInstanceState) <= 256)`. `SAMPLE_COUNT = 32` is **locked** (not a prototype tuning knob): at 32 samples, interpolation error ≤ 0.001 (5× under `CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE = 0.005`); total struct size is **188 bytes** (60 scalar + 128 Samples), satisfying the ≤ 256 ceiling with 68 bytes headroom. `SAMPLE_COUNT = 16` is NOT a valid alternative — interpolation error ≈ 0.003 (66% of tolerance, unsafe margin with no safety headroom for authoring variation). `SAMPLE_COUNT = 64` violates the static_assert (316 bytes > 256). The locked value 32 is an implementation constant; it is not exposed to tuners.

**Upstream — Run State Machine.** Pull-Wave reads `RSM.current_state` and `RSM.is_paused` each tick to gate state advancement (pause-freeze, run-termination drain). Pull-Wave subscribes to `RSM.OnPausedChanged(is_paused: bool)` to detect the pause-resume flush trigger from RUNNING (Rule 19). Pull-Wave does NOT call RSM directly; the RSM→DEAD transition on a hit is Collision GDD's responsibility.

**Upstream — DPC.** Pull-Wave reads `DPC.GetCurrentFrameState().telegraph_window_s` ONCE at SPAWNED entry to capture `lean_duration_s`, then never again. Pull-Wave does NOT read `current_phase`, `wave_spawn_interval_s`, or `max_concurrent_waves` at any time (those are Wave Spawner's domain). `is_active` is read at SPAWNED entry as a sanity check (assert + skip if `is_active == false`).

**Upstream — Player Movement (BINDING FORWARD CONTRACT for PM revision).** Pull-Wave reads `PM.current_lane`, `PM.target_lane`, `PM.movement_state` at LANDED tick. Pull-Wave calls `PM.TriggerNearMissBeat()` on qualifying near-miss. Pull-Wave does NOT subscribe to `PM.OnSlipMidpoint` (R1 RC-A: superseded by direct `PM.current_lane` read; see Rule 11). **PM forward contract (binding for PM revision)**:

1. **5-lane track**: PM Rule 1's 3-lane model (`Left / Center / Right`) must revise to 5 lanes (indices 0–4). `EPlayerLane` enum widens to 5 members OR is replaced by `int32` lane index; `LANE_OFFSET_CM` reorganizes into `LANE_WIDTH_M = 1.0m` (provisional) × signed lane offsets from track center.
2. **SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S safe range tightening**: existing safe range `[0.10, 0.25]s` MUST tighten to `[0.10, 0.15]s`. At `SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S > 0.15s`, the player's lateral reach during `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` drops below the minimum needed to escape worst-case M=3 barrages at `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1` (see F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT in Section D). Cross-system invariant (current form — post-R8 RC-B Option (c) + R10d FLOOR raise): `SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S × MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS + REACTION_BUDGET ≤ TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S`, where `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2` (R8 RC-B Option (c) — registry-locked; decoupled from `MAX_PULLS_PER_BARRAGE = 3`) and `REACTION_BUDGET = 0.20s` default / safe-range ceiling 0.25s (mobile recognition floor; R9 cross-system coordination). At R10d defaults (FLOOR = 0.70s 2026-06-17): `0.15 × 2 + 0.20 = 0.50 ≤ 0.70` ✓ 200ms margin at REACT default; `0.15 × 2 + 0.25 = 0.55 ≤ 0.70` ✓ 150ms margin at REACT=0.25 ceiling. Historical pre-R8 RC-B form (`MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = MAX_PULLS_PER_BARRAGE = 3`, path (b)-resolved FLOOR=0.65s default: `0.15 × 3 + 0.20 = 0.65 ≤ 0.65` zero margin) is retired by R8 RC-B Option (c). Wave Spawner cook-time tooling SHOULD mirror the current invariant on barrage patterns.
3. **`current_lane` semantic during SLIPPING (R1 RC-A binding)**: PM Rules 4 + 7 must continue to guarantee `current_lane` returns the SOURCE lane throughout the SLIPPING state (reassignment to `target_lane` happens only at SLIPPING→SETTLED completion, per PM Rule 4 line 84). This single semantic is the entire interface Pull-Wave's near-miss detection consumes. If PM revision changes this semantic (e.g., reassigning `current_lane` at tween midpoint), Pull-Wave Rule 11 must be revised in lockstep — surfaces as cross-system regression.
4. **`OnSlipMidpoint` delegate**: NO LONGER REQUIRED BY PULL-WAVE. PM may retain or remove the delegate at its own discretion (no other current consumer is documented; verify at PM revision). If retained, the existing signature `(source: EPlayerLane, target: EPlayerLane)` and `TweenProgress = 0.5` firing time are not load-bearing for Pull-Wave.

**Upstream — Wave Spawner forward contract for PEAK identity (R1 RC-D CD adjudication).** Rule 7 (phase-independent wave behavior) is preserved at the wave level: a wave's velocity, lean duration, and trajectory are constant for its lifetime regardless of run-phase changes. This raises a question — **what makes a PEAK wave feel like a PEAK wave** if individual wave behavior is phase-independent? The answer is binding on Wave Spawner, NOT on Pull-Wave:

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — PEAK pattern-pool velocity floor**: Wave Spawner's PEAK pattern pool MUST author per-pattern `ForwardVelocityMs` values whose pool average is ≥ **1.5× the OPENER pool average**. Concretely: if OPENER avg is `6.0 m/s`, PEAK avg must be ≥ `9.0 m/s`. The 1.5× ratio is the minimum coefficient at which PEAK waves read as visibly faster than OPENER waves at typical 6-inch mobile viewing distance, after accounting for `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M = 15.0m` perspective compression. Wave Spawner cook-time tooling SHOULD assert this ratio across the authored PEAK and OPENER pools (admission-time validation, not runtime).

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — uniform barrage velocity (R4 RC-R3-5 — closes OQ-PW-2)**: Wave Spawner MUST NOT emit a barrage (the cluster of onsets emitted within `BARRAGE_SIMULTANEITY_WINDOW_S`) whose member waves have differing `ForwardVelocityMs` values. All members of a single barrage MUST share identical `ForwardVelocityMs` (literal `float` equality, no tolerance). Rationale: F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT computes the escape budget assuming a single `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` covers all barrage onsets; mixed velocities produce staggered `TravelDurationS` per member, breaking the worst-case analysis (one member may land before the player completes the slip chain for the others). Cook-time tooling MUST reject barrage patterns with non-uniform velocity at admission. Distinct barrage patterns within the same phase pool MAY use different uniform velocities — the constraint is within-barrage, not within-pool.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — OPENER barrage prohibition (R4 RC-R3-5)**: Wave Spawner MUST NOT mark any OPENER-phase pattern as `is_barrage = true`. The first wave of any run, and any OPENER-phase wave, must be a solo onset. Rationale: OPENER waves carry the player's first read of the lean-magnitude tier mapping; a barrage as the introduction overloads the read-and-react loop before the player has resolved tier-1 vs tier-2 discrimination. The Player Fantasy lead-up ("a half-beat of lean … you know which way the iron is about to come for you") requires unambiguous one-wave-at-a-time pacing during OPENER. Cook-time tooling MUST reject any OPENER-pool pattern with `is_barrage = true` at admission.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — OPENER tier-0 first-encounter scaffolding (R6 B2 — NEW; R8 RC-D extension 2026-06-11 — 3-part teaching contract; RC-B Pillar 2 binding)**: Wave Spawner's OPENER pattern pool MUST include at least one tier-0 (`SourceLane == TargetLane`) pattern AND at least one tier-0 pattern MUST appear in the player's first 3 OPENER waves (`OPENER_TIER0_INTRO_MAX_WAVE_INDEX = 3` — tightened from R6 B2 value of 5 by R8 RC-D 3-part teaching contract). **R8 RC-D additional teaching constraints (NEW)**: (i) **Lane-placement constraint** — the introducing tier-0 pattern MUST target the player's current lane (typically lane 2 = center spawn; configurable as `OPENER_TIER0_INTRO_TARGET_LANE = PLAYER_SPAWN_LANE`). Rationale: a tier-0 wave on a non-player lane reads as "wave is committing somewhere else" — the scaffolding signal (brightness ramp without rotation, on THIS lane, demanding YOUR escape) is invisible. The tier-0 boundary case must be UN-AVOIDABLE-by-standing-still to teach the dual-channel encoding. (ii) **Waves 1–2 tier-1 ordering** — the first two OPENER waves MUST be solo tier-1 patterns (1-lane shift) — establishes the rotation-as-commitment model (angle = magnitude, visible lean → visible commit) BEFORE the tier-0 boundary case at wave ≤3 tests the channel-separation hypothesis. If wave 1 or wave 2 were tier-0, the player has no rotation-magnitude prior to interpret tier-0's zero-rotation case against. Rationale: Pull-Wave's dual-channel encoding (angle = magnitude, brightness = phase) — per the Channel-separation rationale below — requires the player to learn that brightness ramps independently of rotation. The 3-part contract (lane-placement targeting player + waves 1-2 tier-1 establishment + tier-0 by wave ≤3) is the Pillar 2 teaching minimum. A tier-0 wave in MID/PEAK without prior scaffolding reads ambiguously as "no wave / mute" against the player's untrained intuition, directly breaking Pillar 2. Cook-time tooling MUST validate (a) presence of at least one tier-0 pattern in OPENER pool, (b) ordering constraint — at least one OPENER pattern with `SourceLane == TargetLane == OPENER_TIER0_INTRO_TARGET_LANE` is admitted within the first 3 OPENER-wave indices, (c) waves 1–2 of the OPENER schedule are solo tier-1 (1-lane shift, `is_barrage = false`, `|TargetLane - SourceLane| = 1`). See AC-PW-OPENER-TIER0-INTRO for the cook-time check (extended at R8 RC-D).

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — PEAK barrage minimum-tier exclusion (R8 RC-E — NEW 2026-06-11; RC-E ease-curve onset-delay scope binding; R10d FLOOR=0.65→0.70s context update 2026-06-17 — exclusion preserved)**: Wave Spawner's PEAK pattern pool MUST NOT emit any `is_barrage = true` pattern whose `lean_magnitude_tier < 2` — i.e., tier-1 (1-lane shift) barrages are forbidden in PEAK; tier-0 barrages are already forbidden by R7 B11 + R7 B12 enumeration (no authorable configurations exist for tier-0 barrages with distinct source lanes). Tier-1 PEAK barrages remain forbidden because the back-loaded `LeanEaseCurve_Canonical` defers tier-1's small (17°) max-angle visible rotation past the survivability budget at FLOOR=0.70s (R10d update — was FLOOR=0.65s pre-R10d; tier-1 PEAK exclusion is INDEPENDENT of the R10d FLOOR raise because tier-1's smaller max angle reaches direction-detection too late even under R10d's wider budget — see EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY + AC-PW-PEAK-BARRAGE-MIN-TIER rationale for the post-R10d arithmetic). Tier-1 solo waves and tier-1 OPENER/MID barrages remain permitted (no concurrent-stimulus survivability pressure on solos; OPENER/MID survivability budget is larger by per-wave cadence). Cook-time tooling MUST reject any PEAK-pool barrage pattern with `lean_magnitude_tier < 2` at admission. F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT is correspondingly scoped to `lean_magnitude_tier ≥ 2` per its scope clarification. See AC-PW-PEAK-BARRAGE-MIN-TIER for the cook-time check.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — Class-3 B11 uniform-tier relaxation (R8 RC-C — NEW 2026-06-11; class-3 pool singleton resolution)**: For PEAK barrage patterns whose K_class taxonomy class is **3** (`even-split-middle-commit` — sole admissible triplet `{0, 2, 4}` post-R7-B8), Wave Spawner MAY relax R7 B11 (uniform within-barrage `lean_magnitude_tier`) and admit mixed-tier barrage configurations on class-3 only. Rationale: class-3 reduces to exactly 1 authorable barrage under combined R7 B8 + R7 B11 + R7 B12 (all three members at tier-0 with sources `{0, 2, 4}`; no tier-1+ source assignments survive B12's distinct-source constraint). Without this relaxation, class-3 holds K=1 patterns — failing the min_patterns_per_class ≥ 2 floor required for pool variety and forcing K_class taxonomy re-derivation. The relaxation localizes a Pillar 2 weakening (mixed-tier barrage degrades the collective dual-channel angle-magnitude read for class-3 specifically) in exchange for class-3 pool variety. **The relaxation applies ONLY to K_class == 3 (triplet `{0, 2, 4}`)**; all other K_classes retain R7 B11 uniform-tier as BLOCKING. Each individual member of a mixed-tier class-3 barrage still encodes angle = magnitude correctly at the per-wave level; only the unified-barrage-tier cue (helpful for batch reading) is sacrificed. Cook-time tooling MUST: (a) admit mixed-tier configurations exclusively for class-3 triplet `{0, 2, 4}`; (b) reject mixed-tier configurations for all other K_classes. See AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K for the extended cook-time check.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — Minimum patterns per K_class (R8 RC-C — NEW 2026-06-11; closes K_class floor=ceiling vacuous-satisfaction gap)**: Wave Spawner's PEAK pattern pool MUST contain at least **`MIN_PATTERNS_PER_K_CLASS = 2`** distinct `is_barrage = true` patterns per admissible K_class (classes 3, 4, 5, 6 post-R7-B8 retirement of classes 1 & 2). Rationale: AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K floor `K_class ≥ 4` equals the post-retirement ceiling (K_class_max = 4); the AC is vacuously satisfied with 1 pattern per class, leaving no frequency or variety protection. The min_patterns_per_class ≥ 2 floor ensures each class encodes at least 2 distinct slip-chain decisions — preventing single-pattern monoculture within a class. Class-3 (post-R8 RC-C B11 relaxation) admits enough mixed-tier patterns to satisfy the floor; classes 4, 5, 6 already contain ≥2 raw triplets each (class-4 = `{0,1,4}, {0,3,4}`; class-5 = `{0,2,3}, {1,2,4}`; class-6 = `{0,1,3}, {1,3,4}`). Cook-time tooling MUST enumerate per-class pattern counts and reject the pool if any admissible class falls below the floor. See AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K for the extended cook-time check.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — Within-barrage uniform lean-tier (R7 B11 — NEW; RC-F Pillar 2 dual-channel binding; level-designer R6 RECOMMENDED escalated)**: Wave Spawner MUST NOT emit a barrage (M ≥ 2) whose member waves have differing `lean_magnitude_tier` values. All members of a single barrage MUST share identical `lean_magnitude_tier` (e.g., a tier-2 barrage with all members at 2-lane shift magnitude; a tier-4 barrage with all members at 4-lane shift magnitude). Rationale: Pull-Wave's dual-channel encoding (angle = magnitude, brightness = phase — see Channel-separation rationale in Visual/Audio section) assumes the player forms a SINGLE escape decision per barrage from the barrage's collective angular read. A mixed-tier barrage presents simultaneously-leaning waves with different rotation magnitudes + identical brightness ramps; the brightness channel fails to disambiguate which wave to read first AND the angular channel reads as inconsistent ("which is the tier-4? the one leaning 28° or the one leaning 22°?"). This breaks the Pillar 2 "read the lean, bend the other way" contract. The uniform-tier constraint is stricter than the within-barrage uniform-velocity constraint (forward contract (d), R4 RC-R3-5) but follows the same rationale: barrages are designed-as-a-single-beat; tier mixing turns one beat into M independent reads the player must serialize. Cook-time tooling MUST validate uniform tier at barrage admission. See AC-PW-BARRAGE-UNIFORM-TIER for the cook-time check. Distinct barrage patterns within the same phase pool MAY use different uniform tiers — the constraint is within-barrage only.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — Within-barrage distinct source lanes (R7 B12 — NEW; RC-F Pillar 5 binding; level-designer R6 RECOMMENDED escalated)**: Wave Spawner MUST NOT emit a barrage whose member waves share source lanes. All members of a single barrage MUST have distinct `SourceLane` values. Rationale: barrage members materialize at the SPAWNED → LEANING transition (mesh opacity = 0 during SPAWNED tick; visible from LEANING entry). If multiple barrage members share a source lane, multiple voxel masses pop visible from the same spatial origin in the SPAWNED frame → players cannot read independent threats from a single-spatial-origin pile. Pillar 5 binding ("Skill Is Visible — no mystery deaths") requires that each threat's spatial source be readable independently. Multi-wave SPAWNED-frame pile at one source lane defeats this. The forward contract (h) constraint preserves "one body per source lane at LEANING onset" spatial legibility. Cook-time tooling MUST validate distinct source lanes at barrage admission. See AC-PW-BARRAGE-DISTINCT-SOURCE-LANES for the cook-time check. Note: this is independent of `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1` which constrains TARGET lanes (R2 Cluster E semantic); source-lane distinctness is a parallel constraint on the orthogonal axis.

- **Wave Spawner forward contract — PEAK barrage adjacent-cluster exclusion (R7 B8 — NEW R7; promoted ADVISORY → BLOCKING by 2026-06-11 cross-system survivability coordination Option (c); RC-D Pillar 5 binding + R9 cross-system survivability binding)**: Wave Spawner's PEAK pattern pool MUST NOT emit any M=3 barrage whose three target lanes form an **all-adjacent-cluster triplet** — i.e., three consecutive lane indices `{n, n+1, n+2}` for any `n ∈ {0, 1, 2}` (the three concrete triplets prohibited: `{0,1,2}`, `{1,2,3}`, `{2,3,4}`). **Authoritative 7 surviving M=3 triplets (R9 coordination — Wave Spawner pattern library authoring forward contract)**: `{0,1,3}`, `{0,1,4}`, `{0,2,3}`, `{0,2,4}`, `{0,3,4}`, `{1,2,4}`, `{1,3,4}`. The K_class taxonomy covers these 7 raw triplets across 4 mechanically-distinct escape-strategy classes (classes 3, 4, 5, 6 — see AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K). Rationale (dual binding):
   1. **R7 B8 original rationale (game-designer Hick's Law analysis, preserved)**: M=3 PEAK barrage is a **choice-reaction task** with three simultaneous stimuli; Hick's Law adds ~50-80ms over solo-stimulus baseline at N=3 choice alternatives. The all-adjacent-cluster configurations present three target stimuli AND maximum escape distance simultaneously (worst-case for Hick's Law adjustment). Excluding them reduces the effective choice-alternative count at any player-lane to ≤2 stimuli in the immediate decision space.
   2. **R9 cross-system survivability binding (2026-06-11 — BLOCKING promotion rationale)**: under the F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT at REACT safe-range ceiling 0.25s, all three surveyed cross-system paths break at M=3 unless adjacent-cluster triplets are excluded:
      - Path (a) [tighten SLIP_TWEEN to 0.10]: at REACT=0.25 → `0.10 × 3 + 0.25 = 0.55s ≤ 0.65s` — survives but RELOCATES the cliff (collapses PM tuning headroom and shifts boundary to a different REACT value rather than eliminating it).
      - Path (b) [raise FLOOR to 0.68]: `0.15 × 3 + 0.25 = 0.70s > 0.68s` — **breaks invariant** at REACT=0.25.
      - Path (c) [this contract — cook-time consecutive-triplet exclusion]: across the 7 surviving M=3 triplets × 5 player lanes, worst-case escape distance = 2 slips (creative-director enumeration 2026-06-11); the invariant closes uniformly across the REACT safe range at `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2` (registry constant): `0.15 × 2 + 0.25 = 0.55s ≤ 0.65s` ✓ 100ms margin.
   The R9 binding promotes the gate level from R7 B8's original ADVISORY-until-WS-authored to **BLOCKING immediately** (the upstream forward contract is binding now, not deferred). Cook-time tooling MUST validate this exclusion at PEAK pool admission. See AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER for the cook-time check (R9 coordination promotes this AC to BLOCKING and adds the R7-B8 tag). **Wave Spawner & Pattern Library GDD (#7 in systems-index, Not Started)** inherits the 7-surviving-triplet list as a pre-authoring forward contract — the pattern library must enumerate patterns from these 7 triplets only. **Alternative paths considered & rejected**: re-deriving REACTION_BUDGET against choice-reaction literature (would raise budget cascade DPC), raising TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S directly to 0.75s (DPC cascade rejected at R9 in favor of Option (c)'s smaller blast radius). Reversing this contract requires re-running the 7-triplet × 5-lane enumeration AND re-deriving `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS` (current value 2 depends on this contract).

PEAK identity is therefore communicated through (a) Wave Spawner's per-phase cadence (DPC's `wave_spawn_interval_s` narrows toward PEAK), (b) Wave Spawner's per-phase velocity floor (this contract), and (c) Wave Spawner's per-phase `is_barrage` density (PEAK ≥1–3 barrages per Pillar 2 binding in DPC R5+; OPENER `is_barrage = true` forbidden). Pull-Wave Rule 7 is intentionally agnostic to all three; the variety lives in the pattern pools, not in per-wave behavior. Uniform-within-barrage velocity is the only velocity-shape rule Pull-Wave imposes; the pool-average ratio rule above is the only inter-phase shape rule.

**Downstream — Telegraph System (R6 B5 — single-unregister contract clarified).** Reads `lean_progress` + `target_lane` during LEANING only via a per-tick poll OR subscription to `OnLeanProgress` (Telegraph GDD decides). Telegraph System owns the visual presentation of the lean (tilt angle, voxel deformation, color shift); Pull-Wave owns the behavioral state. **Per-tick read cessation at LEANING exit:** Telegraph stops reading `lean_progress` and stops rendering the lean visual when the wave transitions LEANING → TRAVERSING (or LEANING → DESPAWNING via pause-flush). The Telegraph-side per-tick poll OR `OnLeanProgress` subscription ceases at this boundary — Telegraph does NOT call `UnregisterWave` here. **Single `UnregisterWave` call site at DESPAWNING:** the wave's Telegraph registration slot persists from SPAWNED entry through LEANING / TRAVERSING / LANDED until DESPAWNING. Rule 13 step 2 (`OnTelegraphUnregistered(wave_id)` → `Telegraph->UnregisterWave(wave_id)`) is the **single, sole `UnregisterWave` call site** for any wave; no double-unregister contract is needed because the call fires exactly once per wave_id at the DESPAWNING-entry tick. This resolves R6 B5 — line 204 prior text "Telegraph System unsubscribes at LEANING exit" was ambiguous between "per-tick read cessation" (correct interpretation) and "UnregisterWave call at LEANING exit" (would be a double-unregister bug). The corrected reading: per-tick reads cease at LEANING exit; UnregisterWave fires once at DESPAWNING.

**Downstream — Collision & Hit Detection.** Subscribes to Pull-Wave's per-instance lifecycle. Collision unregisters at DESPAWNING entry (Rule 13 pipeline step 1). Subscribes to `OnWaveHit` to drive RSM→DEAD. Per Collision GDD post-RUNNING hit policy (DPC R6 CD-bound Option B): hits during run-termination drain are scored but cannot flip the terminal RSM state.

**Downstream — Audio Controller, VFX, Camera.** Subscribe to `OnWaveHit`, `OnNearMiss`, `OnWaveDespawned`. None of these systems read Pull-Wave state directly; all reactions are event-driven. Subscriber set is open — Pull-Wave does not enumerate subscribers.

**Downstream — Death Replay.** Captures `FPullWaveSpawnParams` at SPAWNED entry + `OnWaveHit` / `OnNearMiss` events. Reconstructs trajectories deterministically from captured params per Rule 14. No per-frame state capture required.

**Tick ordering.** Pull-Wave ticks AFTER PM (PM commits collision geometry first) and AFTER DPC (DPC snapshot reads must reflect the current frame). Pull-Wave ticks BEFORE Telegraph System (Telegraph reads Pull-Wave's `lean_progress`). Required ordering: `RSM → DPC → PM → Pull-Wave → Telegraph → Collision`. Bound by the OQ-7 ADR's tick-prerequisite mechanism (same ADR that resolves DPC's RSM dependency).

## Formulas

### Shared Variables

These variables appear in two or more formulas below. Values are defined here once; individual formula tables reference them by name.

| Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| `LANE_WIDTH_M` | float | 1.0 m (provisional) | World-space width of each lane in meters. Provisional pending PM revision; defined in PM's `LANE_OFFSET_CM` contract once PM is updated to 5-lane model. |
| `NUM_LANES` | int | 5 (locked) | Total lane count. Locked by Rule 2 (R1 CD adjudication; supersedes R1-draft 6). |
| `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` | float | 10.0–25.0 m; default 15.0 m | Distance in the local-Z axis (toward camera) from the player plane to the wave spawn plane. Tuning knob; see Tuning Knobs section. |
| `PLAYER_PLANE_Z` | float | 0.0 m (convention) | Local-Z coordinate of the player's fixed position in the moving-world frame (Option C — PM OQ-2). The track and hazards translate toward the camera; the player actor sits at Z = 0 in track-local space. |
| `PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MIN_MS` | float | 4.0 m/s | Minimum authored `ForwardVelocityMs` for any pull-wave pattern asset. Enforced by Wave Spawner cook-time validation. |
| `PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MAX_MS` | float | 18.0 m/s | Maximum authored `ForwardVelocityMs` for any pull-wave pattern asset. Enforced by Wave Spawner cook-time validation. |

---

#### F-TRAJ-TNORM: Normalized Traverse Time

```
t_norm = clamp(TraverseElapsedS / TravelDurationS, 0.0, 1.0)
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|----------|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| Accumulated traverse time | `TraverseElapsedS` | float | [0.0, TravelDurationS] s | Per-instance accumulator (canonical time-source post-R2 Cluster A). Initialized to 0.0 at TRAVERSING entry; incremented by `DeltaTime` each TRAVERSING tick where `RSM.GetIsPaused() == false`; held at `TravelDurationS` during LANDED. Never written outside TRAVERSING/LANDED states. See FPullWaveInstanceState field comment for the full lifecycle contract. |
| Traverse duration | `TravelDurationS` | float | (0.0, ∞) s | See F-TRAVERSE-DURATION. Must be > 0; enforced at spawn. |
| Normalized time | `t_norm` | float | [0.0, 1.0] | Output. 0.0 = start of TRAVERSING; 1.0 = trajectory complete. Clamped both ends. |

**Output Range:** Clamped to [0.0, 1.0]. Pre-clamp values above 1.0 are structurally impossible at tick time because the accumulator is capped at `TravelDurationS` at LANDED entry (clamp absorbs any frame-hitch overshoot once), and the tick gate exits before any further increment during the LANDED hold; the output clamp is retained as a defensive guard. Pre-clamp values below 0.0 are structurally impossible because `TraverseElapsedS` initializes to 0.0 at TRAVERSING entry and increments only by non-negative `DeltaTime`.

**Valid only when:** State == `TRAVERSING` or State == `LANDED`. `TravelDurationS > 0.0` (enforced at SPAWNED entry — zero-velocity wave is a cook-time tooling error). `TraverseElapsedS` is undefined (do not read) during SPAWNED, LEANING, or DESPAWNING.

**Tick-order contract (AC-PW-12 reachability).** `t_norm = 0.0` at TRAVERSING entry is reachable **if and only if** the accumulator is read before it is incremented on the first TRAVERSING tick. Required Pull-Wave tick body ordering (each wave, each TRAVERSING tick): (1) read `TraverseElapsedS` → evaluate F-TRAJ-TNORM → evaluate F-TRAJ-LATERAL + F-TRAJ-FORWARD → write `WorldX` / `WorldZ`; (2) THEN increment `TraverseElapsedS += DeltaTime`. If the accumulator were incremented first, `t_norm` on the first TRAVERSING tick would equal `DeltaTime / TravelDurationS` (~0.017 at 60 fps) and AC-PW-12's entry condition would be unreachable again. This GDD declares the required order; enforcement is the implementer's responsibility.

**Determinism note.** `TraverseElapsedS` is `Σ DeltaTime` over TRAVERSING ticks where the pause gate did not fire. For identical DeltaTime sequences, trajectories are bit-identical (Rule 14). `TraverseElapsedS` does NOT promise bit-equivalence across runs with non-identical DeltaTime sequences (vsync jitter, frame-pacer noise); neither did the prior `GameTime − SpawnTimeS` model. Pillar 5's "no mystery deaths" guarantee means **identical inputs produce identical outputs**, not bit-equivalence under variable frame timing.

**Pause correctness.** `TraverseElapsedS` is advanced only inside the Pull-Wave tick, which returns early when `RSM.GetIsPaused()` (Rule 3 pause-freeze gate, see line 97). Pause duration is therefore structurally excluded from the accumulator — there is no positional teleport on resume. This is the canonical fix for the R1-draft wall-clock model's EC-STALE-PAUSE-ABORTED symptom (R2 Cluster A finding).

**Example:** TRAVERSING tick 15 (after init tick). `TravelDurationS = 2.0 s`. After 15 ticks at 16.67 ms each, `TraverseElapsedS = 0.250 s` (read before this tick's increment). `t_norm = 0.250 / 2.0 = 0.125`.

**Boundary checks:**

| Input condition | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| TRAVERSING entry tick, accumulator read before increment | `TraverseElapsedS = 0.0` → `t_norm = 0.0` | AC-PW-12 entry condition reachable; requires the tick-order contract above |
| `TraverseElapsedS = TravelDurationS` | `t_norm = 1.0` | Normal trajectory completion; LANDED hold begins |
| `TraverseElapsedS > TravelDurationS` | Structurally impossible at runtime — accumulator clamped at `TravelDurationS` at LANDED entry, tick gate exits before any further increment | Output clamp to 1.0 remains as defensive guard |
| `TraverseElapsedS < 0.0` | Structurally impossible — accumulator initializes to 0.0 and increments only by non-negative `DeltaTime` | Output clamp to 0.0 remains as defensive guard |
| `TravelDurationS → 0` | Division by near-zero; cook-time tooling rejects | Not evaluated at runtime if validation passes |
| Pause-freeze active (`RSM.GetIsPaused() == true`) | `TraverseElapsedS` unchanged from prior tick; `t_norm` unchanged | Rule 3 pause-freeze gate; no teleport on resume |

---

#### F-TRAJ-LATERAL: Lateral World Position

```
source_lane_x  = SourceLane × LANE_WIDTH_M − ((NUM_LANES − 1) × LANE_WIDTH_M / 2)
lateral_offset = CurveSnapshot.EvaluateAt(t_norm) × (TargetLane − SourceLane) × LANE_WIDTH_M
world_x        = source_lane_x + lateral_offset
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|----------|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| Source lane | `SourceLane` | int | [0, 4] | Spawn lane index. 0 = leftmost, 4 = rightmost. |
| Target lane | `TargetLane` | int | [0, 4] | Landing lane index. `const` for wave lifetime. |
| Lateral offset snapshot | `CurveSnapshot.EvaluateAt(t_norm)` | float | [0.0, 1.0] at `t_norm ∈ [0.0, 1.0]` (within tolerance) | Per-instance immutable curve snapshot sampled at SPAWNED (Rule 14 (a)). Endpoint contract (asset-side, pre-snapshot): `GetFloatValue(0.0) = 0.0 ± CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE`, `GetFloatValue(1.0) = 1.0 ± CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE`. Validated by Wave Spawner at admission. |
| Normalized time | `t_norm` | float | [0.0, 1.0] | Output of F-TRAJ-TNORM. |
| Lane width | `LANE_WIDTH_M` | float | 1.0 m (provisional) | See Shared Variables. |
| Lane count | `NUM_LANES` | int | 5 | See Shared Variables. |
| Source lane world X | `source_lane_x` | float | [−2.0, 2.0] m | X coord of source lane center at default `LANE_WIDTH_M`. |
| Lateral offset | `lateral_offset` | float | [−4.02, 4.02] m | Signed lateral displacement; negative when `TargetLane < SourceLane`. Range bounded by `(TargetLane − SourceLane) × LANE_WIDTH_M × CurveSnapshot.EvaluateAt(t_norm)` at the curve-endpoint tolerance: max delta = 4 × 1.0 × (1.0 ± CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE) = ±4.02 (R4 editorial fix; R3-tagged stale `[−4.0, 4.0]` replaced). |
| World lateral position | `world_x` | float | [−2.02, 2.02] m **at LANE_WIDTH_M=1.0m default** (range scales linearly with LANE_WIDTH_M: at safe-range floor 0.75m → [−1.515, 1.515]; at safe-range ceiling 1.5m → [−3.03, 3.03]) | Output. Bounded by track-center extents at default tuning + curve endpoint tolerance (see Output Range note). |

**Output Range:** Under exact authored curves and lane indices [0,4], lane-center-to-lane-center `world_x ∈ [−2.0, 2.0]` m at `LANE_WIDTH_M = 1.0`. With the `CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE = 0.005` allowance, an actual endpoint deviation of `+0.005` on a 4-lane-delta wave would extend `world_x` to `± (2.0 + 0.005 × 4.0 × 1.0) = ± 2.02` m at endpoints — the declared output range is **`[−2.02, 2.02]` m** at default tuning. Curve values exceeding `1.0 + CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE` would push `world_x` further outside track bounds; Wave Spawner endpoint tolerance check guards against gross overshoot at cook time, not runtime. No runtime clamp applied.

**Valid only when:** `SourceLane ∈ [0, 4]`, `TargetLane ∈ [0, 4]`, `CurveSnapshot.SAMPLE_COUNT == 32` (locked; Wave Spawner snapshots at spawn). `SourceLane == TargetLane` is a valid non-lateral pattern: `lateral_offset = 0` for all `t_norm` — wave travels straight forward.

**Example:** `SourceLane = 4`, `TargetLane = 1`, `LANE_WIDTH_M = 1.0`, `t_norm = 0.6`, snapshot returns 0.7. `source_lane_x = 4.0 − 2.0 = 2.0`. `lateral_offset = 0.7 × (−3) × 1.0 = −2.1`. `world_x = 2.0 + (−2.1) = −0.1 m`. Wave is 60% through, currently 0.1 m left of center, heading for `TargetLane = 1` at x = −1.0 m.

**Boundary checks:**

| Input condition | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `SourceLane == TargetLane` | `world_x = source_lane_x` (constant) | Straight-forward wave; valid pattern |
| `TargetLane < SourceLane` | `lateral_offset` negative | Wave moves left; signed delta handles correctly |
| `t_norm = 0.0` | `world_x = source_lane_x` (within ±CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE × |TargetLane−SourceLane| × LANE_WIDTH_M, ≤ ±0.02 m at max delta=4) | Curve endpoint contract: snapshot `Samples[0] ≈ 0.0` |
| `t_norm = 1.0` | `world_x ≈ TargetLane × LANE_WIDTH_M − ((NUM_LANES−1) × LANE_WIDTH_M / 2)` (same tolerance) | Wave lands at target lane center within endpoint tolerance |
| Source `UCurveFloat` null at admission | Wave Spawner rejects pre-snapshot at cook time | Snapshot never produced; `Construct()` never called |

---

#### F-TRAJ-FORWARD: Forward World Position

```
SpawnPlaneZ = PLAYER_PLANE_Z + SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M

world_z = SpawnPlaneZ − t_norm × SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M
        = PLAYER_PLANE_Z + SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M × (1.0 − t_norm)
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|----------|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| Spawn plane Z | `SpawnPlaneZ` | float | (0.0, ∞) m | Local-Z of wave's spawn position in the moving-world frame. Positive Z = further from camera (behind player). Equals `PLAYER_PLANE_Z + SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` by definition; captured at SPAWNED. |
| Normalized time | `t_norm` | float | [0.0, 1.0] | Output of F-TRAJ-TNORM. |
| Spawn plane offset | `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` | float | [10.0, 25.0] m; default 15.0 m | See Shared Variables. Tuning knob. Also equals `TravelDurationS × ForwardVelocityMs` by F-TRAVERSE-DURATION identity. |
| Player plane Z | `PLAYER_PLANE_Z` | float | 0.0 m (convention) | See Shared Variables. |
| World forward position | `world_z` | float | [0.0, SpawnPlaneZ] m | Output. Local-Z of wave in moving-world frame. Monotonically decreasing during TRAVERSING. |

**Output Range:** `world_z` decreases monotonically from `SpawnPlaneZ` (at `t_norm = 0.0`) to `PLAYER_PLANE_Z = 0.0` (at `t_norm = 1.0`). At `t_norm = 1.0`: `world_z = PLAYER_PLANE_Z + SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M × (1.0 − 1.0) = 0.0` — identity holds exactly, no F-TRAVERSE-DURATION round-trip needed. `t_norm` clamping in F-TRAJ-TNORM bounds the output; no runtime clamp on `world_z`. (R2 Cluster A: simplified from the prior `world_z = SpawnPlaneZ − t_norm × TravelDurationS × ForwardVelocityMs` form by substituting the F-TRAVERSE-DURATION identity `TravelDurationS × ForwardVelocityMs = SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` — algebraically exact, removes a round-trip multiply, makes the lerp from `SpawnPlaneZ` to `PLAYER_PLANE_Z` explicit.)

**Valid only when:** Moving-world frame (PM OQ-2 Option C) is the chosen implementation. If Option A or B is selected, coordinate conventions must be re-derived. `SpawnPlaneZ > PLAYER_PLANE_Z` (cook-time tooling enforces `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M > 0`).

**LANDED hold correctness.** During LANDED, `TraverseElapsedS` is held at `TravelDurationS` (FPullWaveInstanceState field comment), so `t_norm = clamp(TravelDurationS / TravelDurationS, 0.0, 1.0) = 1.0`. Therefore `world_z = PLAYER_PLANE_Z = 0.0` for the full `WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S` duration (~9 ticks at 60 fps). The wave does not drift during the hold. The explicit cap of `TraverseElapsedS` at `TravelDurationS` at LANDED entry is the primary guard; the F-TRAJ-TNORM output clamp is a defensive fallback.

**Example:** `SpawnPlaneZ = 15.0 m`, `t_norm = 0.6`. `world_z = 15.0 − 0.6 × 15.0 = 15.0 − 9.0 = 6.0 m`. Wave is 6 m ahead of player plane.

**Boundary checks:**

| Input condition | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `t_norm = 0.0` | `world_z = SpawnPlaneZ` | TRAVERSING just began; wave at spawn plane |
| `t_norm = 1.0` | `world_z = PLAYER_PLANE_Z = 0.0` | Wave reaches player plane; landing trigger fires (Rule 9). Identity cancels exactly. |
| `world_z ≤ PLAYER_PLANE_Z` (threshold-cross) | TRAVERSING → LANDED | Rule 9 threshold-cross handles frame-hitch overshoot |
| `t_norm = 1.0` but `world_z > PLAYER_PLANE_Z` | Not reachable — `world_z = 0.0` is algebraically guaranteed when `t_norm = 1.0` | Authoring error only if `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M ≤ 0` or `PLAYER_PLANE_Z ≠ 0.0` (both cook-time/structural failures) |
| `TraverseElapsedS` not clamped at `TravelDurationS` during LANDED entry | F-TRAJ-TNORM output clamp holds `t_norm = 1.0`, `world_z = 0.0` — no drift | Struct-level cap is primary; output clamp is fallback (line 146 comment) |

---

#### F-TRAVERSE-DURATION: Total Traverse Duration

```
TravelDurationS = SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M / ForwardVelocityMs
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|----------|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| Spawn plane offset | `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` | float | [10.0, 25.0] m; default 15.0 m | Distance from player plane to spawn plane. Tuning knob. |
| Forward velocity | `ForwardVelocityMs` | float | [4.0, 18.0] m/s | Per-pattern; frozen at SPAWNED. |
| Traverse duration | `TravelDurationS` | float | [0.556, 6.25] s | Output. |

**Output Range:**
- Min: `10.0 / 18.0 = 0.556 s` (min offset, max velocity). At default offset 15.0 m: `15.0 / 18.0 = 0.83 s`.
- Max: `25.0 / 4.0 = 6.25 s` (max offset, min velocity). At default offset: `15.0 / 4.0 = 3.75 s`.
- Combined with `LeanDurationS ≥ TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S = 0.70 s` (R10d 2026-06-17 — raised 0.65 → 0.70s; was 0.65s per R5 B1 → was stale 0.6s), total wave lifetime ranges from `1.53 s` to `4.45 s` at default offset (was 1.48-4.40s under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).

**Valid only when:** `ForwardVelocityMs > 0` (cook-time validation). `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M > 0` (structural). Both frozen at SPAWNED entry per Rule 6.

**Example:** `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M = 15.0`, `ForwardVelocityMs = 10.0`. `TravelDurationS = 1.5 s`. Total wave lifetime = 0.70 + 1.5 = 2.20 s (R10d 2026-06-17 — raised 0.65 → 0.70s; was 2.15s under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s per R5 B1 / pre-R5 stale 0.6s).

**Telegraph-vs-velocity check:** At max velocity 18 m/s with `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M = 15.0`, `TravelDurationS = 0.83 s`. The 0.70 s lean phase (R10d 2026-06-17 — raised 0.65 → 0.70s; `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S = 0.70s` per R10d CD ruling path (i); historical: was 0.65s per R5 B1 → was stale 0.6s, then raised to 0.65s at R2 Cluster E path (b)) still precedes TRAVERSING (LEANING is forward-stationary — wave velocity does not affect telegraph time). Telegraph contract holds at all velocities.

**Boundary checks:**

| Input condition | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `ForwardVelocityMs = 4.0` (min), offset 15.0 m | `TravelDurationS = 3.75 s` | Slowest wave |
| `ForwardVelocityMs = 18.0` (max), offset 15.0 m | `TravelDurationS = 0.83 s` | Fastest wave at default offset |
| `ForwardVelocityMs = 18.0`, offset 10.0 m | `TravelDurationS = 0.556 s` | Absolute minimum across full tuning range |
| `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M > 25.0` | Cook-time warning | Waves survive longer than ~7.12 s (= `0.70 + 6.25 + 0.15 + 0.016` at FLOOR=0.70s, min velocity 4.0 m/s, max offset 25.0 m, default WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S, 1-tick DESPAWNING) — affects object pool sizing. R11a-arithmetic 2026-06-18 corrected pre-R2 stale residue `4.35` (= `0.60 + 15.0/4.0` — wrong FLOOR and wrong offset for this row); see EC-WAVE-LIFETIME-OVERRUN 6.95s ceiling at default WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S=0 simplification. |

---

#### F-SLIP-REACH: REMOVED (R1 RC-C C-1)

**The R1-draft `floor(TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S / SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S)` formula has been deleted.** Under IEEE 754 single precision, `floor(0.6f / 0.15f)` evaluates to **3, not 4** (`0.6f / 0.15f` rounds slightly below 4.0), which would collapse the documented "1-slip margin" to zero. The boundary-check tables in the R1 draft were misleading.

The binding cross-system survivability constraint is the **multiplicative** form in F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT below — that form is float-stable (multiplication and ≤ comparison of two values that are exactly representable to the precision the comparison needs). No design-time `floor()` operation is required at any layer (design-time, cook-time, or runtime); F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT is the sole binding statement.

---

#### F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT: Cross-System Escape Guarantee

This is a design-time invariant, not evaluated at runtime. It constrains the PM forward contract and bounds DPC provisional values.

```
SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S × MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS + REACTION_BUDGET ≤ TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S

where:
  MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2   (registry constant; reduced from 3 by 2026-06-11 cross-system
                          coordination resolution Option (c). The consecutive-target-triplet
                          exclusion in AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER (R7 B8 — BLOCKING)
                          eliminates the three M=3 PEAK configurations {0,1,2}, {1,2,3},
                          {2,3,4} that would have required 3 escape slips at edge-lane
                          player positions. Across the 7 surviving M=3 triplets × 5 player
                          lanes (creative-director enumeration 2026-06-11), worst-case
                          escape distance = 2 slips. See AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER
                          + derivation below.)
  REACTION_BUDGET  = 0.20 s default; safe range [0.20, 0.25]s — mobile recognition floor:
                     acquisition + parse + decision + initiation on a 6-inch screen.
                     See Tuning Knobs. Survivability invariant must hold across the full
                     REACT safe range (Pillar 5 — no deaths attributable to degraded
                     attention; per cross-system survivability coordination 2026-06-11).
```

**Scope clarification (R8 RC-E — onset-delay; R10d closure under CD ruling path (i) — FLOOR raised to 0.70s 2026-06-17).** This invariant is scoped to `lean_magnitude_tier ≥ 2` barrages. Tier-1 (1-lane shift) PEAK barrages are excluded from the PEAK pool by Wave Spawner forward contract (see Detailed Design forward contracts — `PEAK barrage minimum-tier exclusion`); tier-1 solo waves and tier-1 OPENER/MID barrages are governed by AC-PW-TIER1-PERCEPTIBILITY (a smaller perceptibility bound that the math closes cleanly under FLOOR=0.70s). **Perceptibility-onset accounting under R10d (binding direction-detection model)**: the invariant's implicit "REACTION_BUDGET starts at lean_progress = 0" assumption is honored by the back-loaded `LeanEaseCurve_Canonical` under the R10d-binding direction-detection (~1°) perceptibility model. Direction-detection is the survivability-critical signal (the player needs to know WHICH WAY to slip, not exactly how far). At 1° threshold the onset is `t_perceptible_direction ≈ 0.13–0.15 s` and the invariant closes under FLOOR=0.70s: `0.15 + 0.20 + 0.30 = 0.65 s ≤ 0.70 s` ✓ 50ms margin at default REACT; `0.15 + 0.25 + 0.30 = 0.70 s ≤ 0.70 s` ✓ zero margin at REACT=0.25 safe-range ceiling. The Pillar 5 binding goal ("no deaths attributable to degraded attention across the full REACT safe range [0.20, 0.25]s") is satisfied at design-time under FLOOR=0.70s. **PROTO-GATE (AC-PW-TIER-DIRECTION-DETECTION-PROTO-GATE) reframed under R10d** as a perceptibility-model confidence check on the binding direction-detection design decision (no longer load-bearing for invariant closure — closure now derives from the FLOOR raise alone). PROTO-GATE failure handling (if magnitude-discrimination is empirically operative rather than direction-detection) is an Alpha-phase defect class to be addressed via a separate CD ruling at that gate, NOT a design-time admitted residual gap. See EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY and AC-PW-TIER-DIRECTION-DETECTION-PROTO-GATE below.

**Derivation of MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2 (2026-06-11 cross-system coordination resolution Option (c) bound):**

`MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1` means distinct **target-lane** indices only — two concurrent barrage onsets must target different lanes; adjacency is permitted (per the entity registry note: *"adjacent lanes are permitted"* at value=1). **Semantic locked: target-lane distinct (R2 Cluster E CD adjudication).** Under MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION=1 alone, C(5,3) = 10 valid M=3 spatial configurations exist; AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER (R7 B8 — BLOCKING under R9 coordination) excludes the three consecutive-triplet configurations `{0,1,2}`, `{1,2,3}`, `{2,3,4}`, leaving **7 surviving M=3 triplets**: `{0,1,3}`, `{0,1,4}`, `{0,2,3}`, `{0,2,4}`, `{0,3,4}`, `{1,2,4}`, `{1,3,4}` (authoritative list — Wave Spawner pattern library forward contract).

The three excluded triplets are the configurations that would have forced 3 consecutive escape slips at an edge-lane player position. Without them, the surviving-triplet × player-lane enumeration (creative-director 2026-06-11) confirms worst-case escape distance ≤ 2 lanes from any player position. Example: triplet `{0,1,3}` with player at lane 0 — safe lanes are {2, 4}; nearest is lane 2; escape = 2 slips. Example: triplet `{1,2,4}` with player at lane 4 — safe lanes are {0, 3}; nearest is lane 3; escape = 1 slip. Worst-case across the full 7 × 5 = 35 (triplet, player-lane) combinations = 2 slips.

General form: `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = max over surviving M=3 triplets × player lanes of (distance to nearest safe lane) = 2`. Holds when `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1` (target-lane adjacency permitted) AND `NUM_LANES = 5` AND AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER excludes consecutive triplets. Any reduction of `NUM_LANES` below 5, raise of `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION` above 1, OR relaxation of AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER requires re-running the worst-case enumeration. **Distinct from M = `MAX_PULLS_PER_BARRAGE`**: M (barrage size) remains 3; the worst-case-escape decoupling from M is precisely what Option (c) cook-time exclusion bought.

**REACTION_BUDGET decomposition (mobile recognition floor):**

The player's time budget between telegraph-read and slip-completion is not purely locomotion. On a 6-inch mobile touchscreen the full response chain is:

| Component | Typical floor | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Visual acquisition | ~0.07 s | Eyes locate lean direction on screen (saccade + fixation on a peripheral cue; Carpenter (1988), Rayner (1998) eye-movement literature; values consistent with mobile-game empirical floor reported in Mark et al. (2008) and Mathôt et al. (2014) attention-shift studies) |
| Parse + decision | ~0.08 s | Identify target lane, decide slip direction + count (Hick's Law at 5 lanes: H(5) ≈ 2.3 bits × ~30 ms/bit + 10 ms classification = ~80 ms; cf. Hick 1952, Welford 1980) |
| Motor initiation | ~0.05 s | Swipe initiation latency on capacitive touch (capacitive-touch ATD + finger-flexion premotor delay; Wood & Zaichkowsky (2002) HCI touch-latency measurements, ~50 ms on modern smartphone hardware) |
| **Total (REACTION_BUDGET)** | **~0.20 s** | **Provisional minimum; sourced from compositional sum of the three component literatures above. Cannot be reduced without Player Fantasy revision OR platform-specific playtest evidence that SLIPSTORM's pre-telegraphed pattern structure meaningfully reduces acquisition latency below the floor.** |

This budget is consumed BEFORE the first slip tween begins. It is NOT included in `SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S` × `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS`, which counts only locomotion time. The total demand on `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` is therefore reaction + locomotion, not locomotion alone.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|----------|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| Slip tween duration | `SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S` | float | [0.10, 0.15] s | PM safe-range (tightened by this invariant). The locomotion-time variable. |
| Min escape slips | `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS` | int | **2** (registry constant; reduced from 3 by 2026-06-11 cross-system coordination resolution Option (c)) | Worst-case slips across the 7 surviving M=3 triplets × 5 player lanes after AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER exclusion. **Decoupled from MAX_PULLS_PER_BARRAGE = 3**: barrage size remains 3; the worst-case-escape distance is bounded by which triplets are admissible at cook time, not by M directly. |
| Reaction budget | `REACTION_BUDGET` | float | 0.20 s default; safe range [0.20, 0.25] s | Mobile recognition floor. See Tuning Knobs for safe range and rationale. The invariant must hold across the full safe range. |
| Telegraph window floor | `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` | float | **0.70 s** (R10d binding — raised 0.65 → 0.70s 2026-06-17 per CD ruling path (i); propagates to DPC GDD + registry post-R11-APPROVED via `/propagate-design-change`; historical: raised R2 Cluster E path (b) from prior 0.6s; preserved by 2026-06-11 cross-system coordination Option (c)) | Total time budget for reaction + escape locomotion. |
