**Output:** Boolean invariant. True = survivable on 6-inch mobile hardware with realistic reaction time across the full REACT safe range. False = some valid barrages are structurally unescapable.

**Verification at default tuning (post-R10d 2026-06-17 CD ruling path (i) — FLOOR raised 0.65 → 0.70s):**

- **REACT = 0.20 (default)**: `0.15 × 2 + 0.20 = 0.50 ≤ 0.70` ✓ — invariant holds with **0.20s (200ms) margin** (widened from 150ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).
- **REACT = 0.25 (safe-range ceiling)**: `0.15 × 2 + 0.25 = 0.55 ≤ 0.70` ✓ — invariant holds with **0.15s (150ms) margin / 9 frames at 60fps** (widened from 100ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).

Both endpoints of the REACT safe range survive with R10d's widened margins. Compare to pre-Option-(c) state at M=3: `0.15 × 3 + 0.20 = 0.65 ≤ 0.70` would now hold mathematically at 50ms margin (was zero-margin under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s) at REACT=0.20; `0.15 × 3 + 0.25 = 0.70 ≤ 0.70` would now hold mathematically at zero margin (was failed by 50ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s) at REACT=0.25 — but MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS=2 remains the binding closure form (locked by Option (c) + R7 B8 + AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER); R10d FLOOR raise is NOT an M=3 unlock, only a safety-margin widening for the M=2 binding form. Option (c) eliminated the M=3 cliff via cook-time triplet exclusion; R10d adds an additional 50ms perceptibility-onset safety net atop Option (c)'s closure.

**Cross-system coordination resolution (2026-06-11 Option (c) — binding; supplemented by 2026-06-17 R10d CD ruling path (i)):** Four paths surveyed at PM R9 cross-system review + R10d adjudication:
- **Option (a)** tighten SLIP_TWEEN safe range to {0.10s}: at REACT=0.25 → `0.10 × 3 + 0.25 = 0.55 ≤ 0.65` ✓ 100ms margin under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s, **but cliff RELOCATED** — collapses PM tuning headroom and shifts boundary to a different REACT value rather than eliminating it. Rejected.
- **Option (b)** raise `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` to 0.68s: at REACT=0.25 → `0.15 × 3 + 0.25 = 0.70 > 0.68` **breaks invariant**. Survives at REACT=0.20 default only under M=3. Rejected. (Historical note: Option (b)'s "raise FLOOR" direction was correct under M=3 but the magnitude was too small; R10d's path (i) raise to 0.70s under M=2 is the version that closes the post-Option-(c) onset-inclusive cliff.)
- **Option (c)** Wave Spawner cook-time consecutive-target-triplet exclusion (AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER R7 B8 promoted ADVISORY → BLOCKING) reduces worst-case escape to 2 slips: at REACT=0.25 → `0.15 × 2 + 0.25 = 0.55 ≤ 0.65` ✓ 100ms margin under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s. **Closed the M=3 locomotion-only cliff**; preserved at R10d. Preserves PM and DPC tuning (SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S = 0.15s default unchanged; MAX_PULLS_PER_BARRAGE = 3 unchanged); smallest cross-system blast radius for the M=3 closure.
- **R10d CD ruling path (i)** raise `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` to 0.70s (2026-06-17): closes the EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY perceptibility-onset cliff that Option (c)'s locomotion-only form did not address — at REACT=0.25 ceiling + direction-detection model: `0.15 (onset) + 0.25 + 0.30 = 0.70 ≤ 0.70` ✓ zero margin. **Bound (this resolution, supplementing Option (c))**. Cross-system propagation: DPC GDD `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` 0.65 → 0.70s; registry update; AC-PILLAR-2-CONCURRENT re-verify; deferred to `/propagate-design-change` post-R11-APPROVED.

Full adjudication: `design/gdd/reviews/cross-system-survivability-coordination-2026-06-11.md` (creative-director). Specialist convergence: systems-designer empirical boundary math at REACT=0.25 ceiling drove the (a)/(b) elimination; creative-director enumeration of 7 surviving triplets × 5 player lanes confirmed worst-case escape = 2 slips with uniform safety property under (c-actual).

**Boundary checks (post-2026-06-11 cross-system coordination Option (c); post-R10d 2026-06-17 CD ruling path (i) FLOOR raised 0.65 → 0.70s):**

| Tuning condition | Evaluation | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Default — REACT = 0.20**: `SLIP_TWEEN = 0.15`, `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2`, `FLOOR = 0.70` (R10d) | `0.15 × 2 + 0.20 = 0.50 ≤ 0.70` | **Holds — 0.20s margin (200ms / 12 frames at 60fps)** | Active tuning at R10d resolution; widened from 150ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s |
| **REACT safe-range ceiling — REACT = 0.25**: `SLIP_TWEEN = 0.15`, `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2`, `FLOOR = 0.70` (R10d) | `0.15 × 2 + 0.25 = 0.55 ≤ 0.70` | **Holds — 0.15s margin (150ms / 9 frames at 60fps)** | Survivability invariant holds across full REACT safe range — the Pillar 5 binding goal of Option (c), preserved at R10d's wider margin; widened from 100ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s |
| Pre-R10d state at REACT = 0.20 (FLOOR = 0.65, MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2): `0.50 ≤ 0.65` | Held — 150ms margin | Superseded by R10d FLOOR raise | Pre-R10d active tuning, documented for traceability |
| Pre-R10d state at REACT = 0.25 ceiling (FLOOR = 0.65, MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2): `0.55 ≤ 0.65` | Held — 100ms margin (but EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY perceptibility-onset analysis showed an EFFECTIVE 50ms cliff at REACT ceiling under direction-detection: `0.15 + 0.25 + 0.30 = 0.70 > 0.65`) | Superseded by R10d FLOOR raise | The EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY perceptibility-onset cliff was the R10d trigger; pre-R10d boundary-table math obscured this because the table tracked locomotion-only `SLIP_TWEEN × M + REACT ≤ FLOOR` form, not the onset-inclusive form |
| Pre-Option-(c) state at REACT = 0.20 (MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 3, FLOOR = 0.65): `0.15 × 3 + 0.20` | `0.65 ≤ 0.65` | Held at zero margin | Superseded; documented for traceability |
| Pre-Option-(c) state at REACT = 0.25 (MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 3, FLOOR = 0.65): `0.15 × 3 + 0.25` | `0.70 > 0.65` | **Failed** | Superseded; the cliff that motivated cross-system coordination |
| Pre-Option-(c) state at REACT = 0.25 (MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 3, FLOOR = 0.70 R10d): `0.70 = 0.70` | Holds at zero margin (mathematically; locomotion-only) | NOT reverted to M=3 | R10d FLOOR raise alone would mathematically absorb the M=3 ceiling cliff in the locomotion-only form, but MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS=2 remains the binding closure (locked by R8 RC-B + R7 B8 + AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER 7-surviving-triplet enumeration); the FLOOR raise is the EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY safety net, not an M=3 unlock |
| Option (a) alt at REACT = 0.25: `SLIP_TWEEN = 0.10, MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 3, FLOOR = 0.65` | `0.55 ≤ 0.65` | Holds — 0.10s margin | Rejected — relocates cliff to a different REACT value rather than eliminating it; collapses PM safe range |
| Option (b) alt at REACT = 0.25: `SLIP_TWEEN = 0.15, MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 3, FLOOR = 0.68` | `0.70 > 0.68` | **Failed** | Rejected — invariant remains broken at ceiling; R10d's FLOOR=0.70s subsumes this option's intent at the binding ceiling |
| `SLIP_TWEEN = 0.16`, `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2`, `REACT = 0.25`, `FLOOR = 0.70` (R10d) | `0.57 ≤ 0.70` | Holds — 0.13s margin | Raising SLIP_TWEEN past PM's 0.15s ceiling survives under Option (c) + R10d FLOOR raise — but PM tuning range still locked at [0.10, 0.15]s by AC-PW-SLIP-TWEEN-CONSISTENCY |
| `SLIP_TWEEN = 0.15`, `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2`, `REACT = 0.30`, `FLOOR = 0.70` (R10d) | `0.60 ≤ 0.70` | Holds — 0.10s margin | Raising REACT past 0.25 safe-range ceiling survives under R10d with measurable margin; widened from 50ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s |

**Valid only when:** PM Rule 4 zero-delay buffer-flush holds AND AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER is BLOCKING (consecutive-triplet exclusion enforced at cook time). If consecutive triplets re-enter the PEAK pool, `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS` reverts to 3 and the historical cliff returns (under R10d FLOOR=0.70s, the M=3 + REACT=0.25 + locomotion-only case mathematically holds at zero margin per the boundary table — but the onset-inclusive form per EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY still requires AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER to bound choice-reaction load). Inter-slip gate analysis under Option (c) + R10d FLOOR=0.70s: with MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2 the total demand under K=1 baseline gate-augmented chaining is `REACTION_BUDGET + SLIP_TWEEN × 2 + 2 × 1 × frame_dt` (R10c — Pull-Wave R9 qa-lead F6 K-frame form). At REACT=0.25 ceiling at 60fps (`frame_dt = 0.0167s`): `0.25 + 0.30 + 2 × 1 × 0.0167 = 0.583s ≤ 0.70s` — **117ms margin survives** (widened from R10c's 67ms under FLOOR=0.65s; further widening under R10d FLOOR=0.70s). See EC-INTER-SLIP-GATE-INTRODUCTION for full per-framerate analysis and AC-PW-32 for the K-frame multi-frame gate table.

## Edge Cases

**EC-NEARMISS-PREMIDPOINT — REMOVED (R1 RC-A).** The R1-draft edge case ("wave lands before `OnSlipMidpoint` fires") was an artifact of the recording-then-resolve mechanism. After RC-A's switch to **direct read of `PM.current_lane` at LANDED** (see Rule 11), `current_lane` is correct from the moment SLIPPING begins (PM Rules 4 + 7 guarantee source-lane semantics throughout the tween). No premidpoint window exists; no fallback inference is required; no playtest validation is needed for the inference branch. The R1-draft AC for this EC (formerly AC-PW-23) is deleted (see AC section, AC ID retained as DELETED for historical traceability).

**EC-PAUSE-FLUSH** (States and Transitions) — `RSM.OnPausedChanged(is_paused=false)` fires from a RUNNING(paused) state. Per RSM Rule 19, ALL in-flight waves in any non-DESPAWNING state transition immediately to DESPAWNING with `despawn_reason = PauseFlush`. NO hit/near-miss events fire. The pause flush is the only despawn path that drops trajectories mid-flight without resolution. New spawns gate on `resume_grace` clearing.

**EC-STALE-PAUSE-ABORTED** (States and Transitions) — RSM transitions RUNNING(paused) → ABORTED. `is_paused` becomes false but `RSM.OnPausedChanged` does NOT fire from RUNNING (the transition is from PAUSED to ABORTED, not PAUSED to RUNNING). Frozen waves unfreeze (Pull-Wave tick gate `if RSM.GetIsPaused()` no longer triggers). Accumulators resume from their pre-pause values — `LeanProgress` for waves in LEANING at the time of pause, `TraverseElapsedS` for waves in TRAVERSING at the time of pause. Pause duration is NOT included in either accumulator: the accumulator values at the first post-unfreeze tick are exactly the pre-pause values, and advance by `DeltaTime` only from that point forward (R2 Cluster A no-teleport invariant). Trajectory continuity is preserved across the freeze — no positional teleport. Waves drain to TRAVERSING → LANDED → DESPAWNING with `despawn_reason = RunTermination`. LANDED hit/near-miss events fire — distinct from EC-PAUSE-FLUSH.

**EC-RUN-TERMINATION-DRAIN** (Section C Rule 13 + DPC R6 CD-bound) — RSM transitions RUNNING → DEAD/COMPLETE/ABORTED mid-flight. DPC publishes `is_active = false`. In-flight waves continue TRAVERSING → LANDED → DESPAWNING normally with `despawn_reason = RunTermination`. LANDED hit events fire and are bookkept against the just-ended run, but per Collision GDD post-RUNNING hit policy, they CANNOT flip the terminal RSM state (a barrage hit at t=60.3s does not retroactively reclassify a COMPLETE run as DEAD).

**EC-CURVE-NULL-AT-SPAWN** (F-TRAJ-LATERAL boundary) — `LateralOffsetCurve` is null at Wave Spawner's `Construct()` call. Wave Spawner asserts (`check(Params.LateralOffsetCurve != nullptr)`) and rejects the wave at the cook-time / asset-load validation gate. NO runtime fallback path. A wave that reaches SPAWNED with a null curve is a contract violation upstream (cook-time validation failure escaped detection); Pull-Wave's behavior is undefined and the implementation MAY assert (`check(LateralOffsetCurve)`).

**EC-CURVE-OVERSHOOT** (F-TRAJ-LATERAL boundary) — Authored `LateralOffsetCurve` returns a value > 1.0 (or < 0.0) at some interior `t_norm`. Wave's `world_x` exceeds track bounds during TRAVERSING; visually the wave swings off-screen and re-enters. Wave Spawner cook-time tooling SHOULD validate `LateralOffsetCurve.GetFloatValue(t) ∈ [0.0, 1.0]` for `t ∈ [0.0, 1.0]` (with `CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE = 0.005` margin). At runtime, no clamp applied — overshoot is treated as a tooling failure, not a runtime fault. If a future authoring decision permits intentional overshoot (e.g., a "wind-up" fake-out), this EC must be revisited; current contract is "stay in bounds."

**EC-CURVE-ENDPOINT-DRIFT** (F-TRAJ-LATERAL boundary) — `LateralOffsetCurve.GetFloatValue(1.0)` returns a value outside `1.0 ± CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE`. Wave does not land at `TargetLane`'s exact center. Wave Spawner cook-time tooling rejects at admission. Same response as EC-CURVE-OVERSHOOT — tooling failure, not runtime fault.

**EC-ZERO-OR-NEGATIVE-VELOCITY** (F-TRAVERSE-DURATION boundary) — Authored `ForwardVelocityMs ≤ 0`. `TravelDurationS = SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M / ForwardVelocityMs` is undefined (zero-divide) or negative. Wave Spawner cook-time tooling rejects (`assert: ForwardVelocityMs ∈ [PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MIN_MS, PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MAX_MS]`). No runtime fallback.

**EC-LANDING-OVERSHOOT-FORWARD** (F-TRAJ-FORWARD + Rule 9) — On a frame-hitch, `world_z` advances beyond the player plane in a single frame (`world_z << 0.0`). Rule 9's threshold-cross (`world_z ≤ player_plane_z`) fires correctly regardless of overshoot magnitude — LANDED still triggers, collision check still fires at the correct tick. The wave's lateral position at the trigger tick is the position evaluated AT the overshoot frame, NOT interpolated back to exactly `world_z = 0`. Acceptable: lateral curve is smooth and the lateral position at one-frame overshoot is within `lateral_offset_per_frame ≤ ForwardVelocityMs × DeltaTime × (lateral velocity / forward velocity)` ≈ <0.1 lane at any realistic frame rate.

**EC-DPC-INACTIVE-AT-SPAWN** (Section C Interactions — DPC binding) — Wave Spawner calls `Construct()` for a wave but `DPC.GetCurrentFrameState().is_active == false` (DPC published inactive snapshot mid-spawn-decision, e.g., RSM is_paused flipped to true within the same tick). SPAWNED-state init asserts `is_active == true` (sanity check); on failure, the wave is rejected, no SPAWNED-entry registered, Wave Spawner restores the slot. Defensive — the Wave Spawner gate on `is_active` at admit time should already have caught this; the SPAWNED-entry assert is belt-and-suspenders.

**EC-FORCED-DESPAWN-NO-EVENT** (Section C Rule 13) — Wave reaches DESPAWNING via any forced path (PauseFlush or RunTermination from a non-LANDED state). No `OnWaveHit` or `OnNearMiss` event is published. Only `OnWaveDespawned(wave_id, despawn_reason, final_lane)` fires. Subscribers (Audio, VFX, Camera) that infer wave outcome from non-firing events must treat the absence of OnWaveHit/OnNearMiss followed by OnWaveDespawned with `despawn_reason ≠ NaturalLanding` as a forced despawn — clean any subscriber-side wave-bound effects (e.g., approaching-wave audio cue) without playing the resolution beat.

**EC-CONCURRENT-LANDING-ON-SAME-LANE** (Section C Rule 12 — no cross-wave physics) — Two waves with identical `target_lane` land on the same tick (barrage authoring permits this with M ≤ 3 onsets within W = 0.35s; 2026-06-19 /propagate-design-change post-R14 — W flipped 0.3s → 0.35s to restore FLOOR/2 design ratio at FLOOR=0.70s; if forward velocities are also identical, simultaneous LANDED entry is reachable). Hit and near-miss resolution per Rule 10/11 evaluates independently for each wave — both fire `OnWaveHit` or `OnNearMiss` separately. Collision GDD's subscriber must handle two same-tick hits: typically the first hit triggers RSM→DEAD and the second hit is bookkept against the just-died run (per Collision post-RUNNING policy). No deduplication at Pull-Wave layer.

**EC-WAVE-LIFETIME-OVERRUN** (F-TRAVERSE-DURATION + Tuning Knobs) — `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` tuned above 25 m AND `ForwardVelocityMs` at floor (4.0 m/s). Wave lifetime exceeds 6.95 s (= 0.70 + 25/4; R10d — FLOOR raised 0.65 → 0.70s 2026-06-17; was 6.90s under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s per R5 B1). Wave Spawner's object pool must size to accommodate the maximum simultaneous in-flight count (≤ MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP = 16) × maximum wave lifetime. Cook-time tooling SHOULD warn if any pattern asset produces TravelDurationS > 5.0 s; the warning is informational, not blocking. The +50ms lifetime ceiling shift is informational for Wave Spawner pool sizing (forward contract — verify against MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP=16 sizing math when Wave Spawner GDD is authored).

**EC-INTER-SLIP-GATE-INTRODUCTION** (F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT — **R8 RC-B post-Option-(c) inversion; R10c — Pull-Wave R9 qa-lead F6 K-frame form 2026-06-17; R10d — FLOOR raised 0.65 → 0.70s 2026-06-17, per-framerate margins re-derived**) — PM author introduces a K-frame settled gate between chained slips (violating current PM Rule 4 zero-delay buffer-flush invariant). Per F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT post-2026-06-11 cross-system coordination Option (c) + R10c's K-frame restatement + R10d FLOOR raise, the total demand under gate-augmented chaining is `REACTION_BUDGET + SLIP_TWEEN × MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS + MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS × K × frame_dt` (K = inter-slip gate frames-per-slip; K=1 is the baseline single-frame gate; frame_dt per fps: 60fps → 0.0167s, 30fps → 0.0333s). **At R10d defaults (`SLIP_TWEEN = 0.15s`, `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS = 2`, `REACTION = 0.20s`, `FLOOR = 0.70s`) the K=1 baseline invariant passes with 200ms margin; an inter-slip gate at typical frame rates survives with substantial margin.** Per-framerate worked examples at K=1 baseline + REACT=0.20:
- 60 fps: `0.20 + 0.30 + 2 × 1 × 0.0167 = 0.533s ≤ 0.70s` — **holds with 167ms margin (≈10 frames)** (widened from 117ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).
- 30 fps: `0.20 + 0.30 + 2 × 1 × 0.0333 = 0.567s ≤ 0.70s` — **holds with 133ms margin (≈4 frames at 30Hz)** (widened from 83ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).

At REACT=0.25 safe-range ceiling:
- 60 fps: `0.25 + 0.30 + 2 × 1 × 0.0167 = 0.583s ≤ 0.70s` — **holds with 117ms margin (≈7 frames)** (widened from 67ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).
- 30 fps: `0.25 + 0.30 + 2 × 1 × 0.0333 = 0.617s ≤ 0.70s` — **holds with 83ms margin (≈2 frames at 30Hz)** (widened from 33ms / one-frame under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s).

The pre-Option-(c) conclusion "any inter-slip gate at any frame rate immediately violates" is **inverted** by the cross-system coordination — at MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS=2 + R10d FLOOR=0.70s the K=1 budget absorbs one inter-slip frame at both 60fps AND 30fps with ≥2-frame margin even at REACT ceiling. **R10d practical-binding update**: the pre-R10d "30fps + REACT=0.25 has only 1-frame (33ms) margin" K=1 binding ceiling case is now retired — the 30fps K=1 case has 2+ frames of margin under R10d. The new K=1 binding ceiling case is 30fps + REACT=0.25 at 83ms margin; the new K=2 binding ceiling case is 30fps + REACT=0.25 at 17ms (per AC-PW-32 K=2 30fps cell — 1-frame margin = effectively zero practical margin once frame-pacer jitter, vsync stalls, or platform-level deferred frames enter the system). If PM introduces K≥2 multi-frame inter-slip gates at 30fps OR if a future revision raises MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS back to 3, see AC-PW-32 for the K-frame multi-frame gate table (K=1/2/3/≥4 × 60fps/30fps under REACT=0.25 ceiling and FLOOR=0.70s); flagged to PM and DPC as a cross-system constraint review trigger on any such change.

**EC-TELEGRAPH-FLOOR-LOWERED-BELOW-SURVIVABILITY-THRESHOLD** (F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT — **R8 RC-B post-Option-(c) threshold update; R10d FLOOR raised 0.65 → 0.70s 2026-06-17 — cushion margins widened**) — DPC's AC-PILLAR-2-CONCURRENT playtest passes at a `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` value below the survivability threshold. Under 2026-06-11 cross-system coordination Option (c) resolution, threshold at REACT safe-range ceiling = `SLIP_TWEEN × MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS + REACTION_BUDGET = 0.15 × 2 + 0.25 = 0.55 s` — i.e., FLOOR cannot drop below 0.55s without breaking F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT at the REACT=0.25 ceiling (the SURVIVABILITY threshold itself is FLOOR-independent — it is a function of `SLIP_TWEEN × MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS + REACTION`, not FLOOR). Threshold at REACT default = `0.15 × 2 + 0.20 = 0.50 s` — the floor below which even the default REACT survivability fails. **R10d FLOOR=0.70s locks in widened cushion margins**: FLOOR sits 150ms above the binding ceiling threshold (widened from 100ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s) and 200ms above the default threshold (widened from 150ms under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s); the additional 50ms cushion is the EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY perceptibility-onset safety net adopted by R10d. The old "BELOW-0.45S" (locomotion-only invariant) and pre-Option-(c) "BELOW-0.65S" (M=3 ceiling) names are retired. **R10d binding note**: FLOOR locked at 0.70s per R10d CD ruling path (i); the historical "raising FLOOR back toward 0.65s if Option (c) is reversed" branch is retained for traceability but is NOT a current resolution path (R10d's FLOOR raise is a one-way decision — DPC's binding value moves to 0.70s; reverting FLOOR would re-open the EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY perceptibility-onset cliff at REACT=0.25 ceiling). F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT requires (a) FLOOR ≥ 0.70s (R10d binding, propagates to DPC post-R11-APPROVED) AND (b) AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER BLOCKING (current state — preserves MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS=2 closure) AND (c) re-running the worst-case enumeration if `NUM_LANES` or `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION` changes. Pull-Wave Open Questions track this as a cross-system risk; resolution path is DPC-side for FLOOR changes, Pull-Wave-side for the consecutive-triplet exclusion.

**EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY** (F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT — **R8 RC-E NEW; R10b interpolation qualifier added 2026-06-16; R10d closure under CD ruling path (i) — FLOOR raised to 0.70s 2026-06-17**) — `LeanEaseCurve_Canonical` control points `(0.0→0.0, 0.4→0.15, 0.7→0.7, 1.0→1.0)` (under R10b-pinned cubic Hermite interpolation with auto tangents — see registry entry `LeanEaseCurve_Canonical` block + §curve description's cook-time fingerprint check (2) at GDD line ~697; linear interpolation is forbidden because it would shift the perceptibility-onset arithmetic below by ~30ms and contradict the fingerprint targets; AC-PW-22b pattern 12 — distinct from this content check — is the source-grep FORBID test for inlined `UCurveFloat*` literals, which is a separate enforcement surface) defer visible rotation past the direction-detection perceptibility threshold (`~1°` rotational delta — the survivability-critical signal under the R10d-binding perceptibility model) until lean_progress ≈ 0.20–0.23, corresponding to `t_perceptible_direction ≈ 0.13–0.15 s` (the load-bearing perceptibility-onset reference for survivability math under FLOOR=0.70s). **R10d CD ruling adopted path (i) — FLOOR raised 0.65 → 0.70s (2026-06-17)**: F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT for tier ≥ 2 barrages closes across the full REACT safe range [0.20, 0.25]s — at REACT=0.20 default: `0.15 (onset) + 0.20 (REACT) + 0.30 (locomotion) = 0.65 ≤ 0.70 s` ✓ (50ms margin); at REACT=0.25 safe-range ceiling: `0.15 + 0.25 + 0.30 = 0.70 ≤ 0.70 s` ✓ (zero margin). The R9 cross-system coordination premise — "the survivability invariant must hold across the full REACT safe range [0.20, 0.25]s; no deaths attributable to degraded attention" — is satisfied at design-time under FLOOR=0.70s; the R8 RC-E residual gap (50ms over budget at REACT=0.25 ceiling under the prior FLOOR=0.65s) is retired. **Binding perceptibility-model design decision (R10d)**: the direction-detection model (~1°) is the BINDING perceptibility floor for F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT closure at design-time. The CD ruling's path (i) selected this model on the basis that the survivability-critical signal under the back-loaded eased curve is *direction* (which way to slip), not *magnitude* (how far) — the player's lateral commitment can begin on direction alone and refine on magnitude during the locomotion budget. **PROTO-GATE reframe (R10d — 2026-06-17)**: AC-PW-TIER-DIRECTION-DETECTION-PROTO-GATE was R8 RC-E's load-bearing closure mechanism under the "document gap as prototype risk" framing — its empirical outcome gated F-invariant closure. Under R10d, PROTO-GATE is reframed from "closure-gate-for-invariant" to "perceptibility-model confidence check on the binding design decision" — F-invariant closes on the FLOOR raise alone (independent of PROTO-GATE outcome), and PROTO-GATE empirically validates that direction-detection is the operative perceptibility model on actual hardware. **PROTO-GATE failure handling (Alpha-phase defect class, NOT a design-time admitted residual gap)**: if PROTO-GATE reveals magnitude-discrimination is the actual operative model rather than direction-detection, this constitutes a prototype-found defect to address at the Alpha gate via a separate CD ruling (escalation paths in that future ruling may include FLOOR raise toward 0.82s, front-load the ease curve, or PM SLIP_TWEEN tightening — but those branches are NOT design-time hedges, they are contingencies on a prototype-phase defect class that R10d explicitly does not pre-author). **Tier-1 PEAK barrage exclusion**: remains BINDING via Wave Spawner forward contract (R8 RC-E + AC-PW-PEAK-BARRAGE-MIN-TIER); tier-1's smaller (17°) max-angle reaches the direction-detection threshold later than tier ≥ 2 under the back-loaded curve, and the exclusion is independent of the R10d FLOOR raise. **Forward contract on DPC** (propagates post-R11-APPROVED via `/propagate-design-change`): `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S` default raised from 0.65s to 0.70s; AC-PILLAR-2-CONCURRENT playtest gate re-verifies against new FLOOR. **OQ-PW-EASE-CURVE-ONSET retained** as the prototype validation reference for the direction-detection model confidence check (PROTO-GATE captures the empirical determination).

## Dependencies

**Upstream (systems this one depends on):**

| System | Hardness | Direction | Interface |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Run State Machine** | Hard | Pull-Wave reads, RSM does not read | Reads `current_state` + `is_paused` each tick (pause-freeze gate). Subscribes to `OnPausedChanged(is_paused: bool)` for pause-resume flush trigger (Rule 19). Does NOT call RSM directly; RSM→DEAD on hit is Collision GDD's responsibility. |
| **Difficulty & Phase Controller** | Hard | Pull-Wave reads, DPC does not read | Reads `DPC.GetCurrentFrameState().telegraph_window_s` ONCE at SPAWNED entry to capture `lean_duration_s`. Does NOT read `current_phase`, `wave_spawn_interval_s`, or `max_concurrent_waves` (Wave Spawner's domain). Reads `is_active` at SPAWNED entry as sanity check. **Inherits forward contracts**: spawn-time velocity binding (no mid-flight update), AC-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K (closed by this GDD: 5-lane × separation=1 = C(5,3)=10 configs ≥ 4 ✓), OQ-DPC-6 derivation (closed: MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1 final). |
| **Player Movement** | Hard | Pull-Wave reads at LANDED only; PM does not read Pull-Wave | Reads `PM.current_lane`, `PM.target_lane`, `PM.movement_state` at LANDED tick (Rule 10 hit resolution + Rule 11 near-miss detection via direct read of `current_lane`). Calls `PM.TriggerNearMissBeat()` on qualifying near-miss. NO subscription to `PM.OnSlipMidpoint` (R1 RC-A — direct read supersedes). **PM forward contract (BINDING for PM revision)**: (1) 5-lane track; (2) `SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S` safe range tightened to `[0.10, 0.15]s`; (3) PM Rules 4 + 7 guarantee `current_lane` returns SOURCE lane throughout SLIPPING (reassignment to `target_lane` at SLIPPING→SETTLED completion only) — this single semantic IS Pull-Wave's near-miss interface; (4) `OnSlipMidpoint` delegate optional (no longer consumed by Pull-Wave). |
| **Wave Spawner & Pattern Library** | Hard | Wave Spawner calls Pull-Wave's `Construct()`; Pull-Wave does not call Wave Spawner (only fires events via `IWaveSpawnerCallback`) | Wave Spawner instantiates each wave via `Construct(FPullWaveSpawnParams)` and validates the params at admission. Produces `FPullWaveCurveSnapshot` from the source `UCurveFloat` asset BEFORE `Construct()` (Rule 14 (a)). Validates `LateralOffsetCurve` endpoints (`CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE = 0.005`) and `ForwardVelocityMs` range. Allocates `wave_id` monotonically (Rule 14 (b)). Enforces `max_concurrent_waves` cap upstream (DPC-bound, ≤16); Pull-Wave does not self-limit. **Wave Spawner forward contracts (BINDING per this GDD)**: (a) PEAK pool avg `ForwardVelocityMs` ≥ 1.5 × OPENER pool avg (R1 RC-D PEAK-identity binding); (b) per-pattern `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION = 1` cook-time enforcement; (c) PEAK pool has **≥6 mechanically-distinct escape-strategy classes** for barrage patterns (R4 RC-R3-5 — strengthened from "≥4 distinct `is_barrage = true` patterns" per R3 CD adjudication #5; see AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K); (d) **uniform barrage velocity** — all members of any single barrage MUST share identical `ForwardVelocityMs` (R4 RC-R3-5 — closes OQ-PW-2); (e) **OPENER `is_barrage=true` forbidden** — no OPENER-phase pattern may be flagged as a barrage; first wave must be solo (R4 RC-R3-5); (f) `wave_id` allocated **monotonically and deterministically** from a documented initial allocator state (R4 RC-R3-4 ↑ on Rule 14(b)). Subscribes to `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnWaveDespawned` (pool return), `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnCollisionUnregistered` (ordering observability — Seam 13), and `IWaveSpawnerCallback::OnTelegraphUnregistered` (R4 RC-R3-4 — Telegraph step (ii) observability). |

**Downstream (systems that depend on this one):**

| System | Hardness | What it reads from Pull-Wave | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Telegraph System** | Hard | `lean_progress` + `target_lane` during LEANING only (per-tick poll or `OnLeanProgress` subscription — Telegraph GDD decides). | Telegraph owns the visual presentation of the lean (tilt angle, voxel deformation, color shift); Pull-Wave owns the behavioral state. Telegraph unsubscribes at LEANING exit. Must NOT impose its own floor below `TELEGRAPH_WINDOW_FLOOR_S = 0.70s` (R10d binding — raised 0.65 → 0.70s 2026-06-17 per CD ruling path (i); DPC binding; historical: raised R2 Cluster E path (b) from prior 0.6s). |
| **Collision & Hit Detection** | Hard | Subscribes to Pull-Wave's per-instance lifecycle; reads wave world position during TRAVERSING + LANDED. | Collision unregisters at DESPAWNING entry (Rule 13 pipeline step 1). Subscribes to `OnWaveHit` to drive RSM→DEAD. Per Collision GDD post-RUNNING hit policy (DPC R6 CD-bound Option B): hits during run-termination drain are scored but cannot flip terminal RSM state. Inherits the post-RUNNING in-flight hit forward contract from DPC R7. **R4 RC-R3-4 forward contract**: `Collision->UnregisterWave(WaveId)` MUST execute synchronously in the SAME TICK as Pull-Wave's `OnCollisionUnregistered` call — deferred-to-next-tick unregister is forbidden (would let a wave's collision volume persist into the next tick after `OnWaveDespawned` fired, opening a one-tick stale-collision window). |
| **Audio Controller** | Soft | Subscribes to `OnWaveHit`, `OnNearMiss`, `OnWaveDespawned` events. | Does not read Pull-Wave state directly; all reactions event-driven. Per forced-despawn EC: must clean wave-bound audio cues without playing resolution beat when `despawn_reason ≠ NaturalLanding`. |
| **VFX / Camera** | Soft | Subscribes to `OnWaveHit`, `OnNearMiss`, `OnWaveDespawned`. Camera also reads via Near-Miss Detection (see below). | Event-driven; same forced-despawn cleanup requirement as Audio Controller. |
| **Near-Miss Detection** | Soft | Reads `OnNearMiss` events (Pull-Wave is the detection source per Rule 11). | Aggregates near-miss events for downstream consumers (scoring, camera shake, etc.). Pull-Wave defines the detection logic; Near-Miss Detection routes the event. |
| **Death Replay** | Soft | Captures `FPullWaveSpawnParams` at SPAWNED entry + `OnWaveHit` / `OnNearMiss` events. | Reconstructs trajectories deterministically from captured params per Rule 14. No per-frame state capture required. |

**Systems that do NOT read Pull-Wave** (explicitly enumerated to prevent accidental coupling): Input System (R-5 forbids it from depending on anything), Run State Machine (Pull-Wave is downstream of RSM, not vice versa), Difficulty & Phase Controller, In-Run HUD, End-Run Screen, Score Persistence, Scoring Logic (reads Collision + Near-Miss, not Pull-Wave directly).

**Bidirectional consistency check (as of this GDD's authoring):**

- **RSM** ↔ Pull-Wave: RSM Rule 19 (pause-flush mechanism) is the contract Pull-Wave EC-PAUSE-FLUSH inherits. RSM lists Pull-Wave as a Rule 19 consumer; verify on RSM revision.
- **DPC** ↔ Pull-Wave: DPC R7 Pull-Wave row (line 595 of difficulty-phase-controller.md) lists the spawn-time velocity binding. DPC R7 Wave Spawner row item (8) + (11) reference `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION` (Pull-Wave-owned, closed at value=1). DPC R7 `OQ-DPC-6` is closed by this GDD's F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT derivation; DPC GDD's open-questions table should remove OQ-DPC-6 on the next consistency-check pass.
- **Player Movement** ↔ Pull-Wave: PM revision is BLOCKING for this GDD's implementation; PM must (a) widen `EPlayerLane` to 5-lane (or replace with `int32` index), (b) tighten `SLIP_TWEEN_DURATION_S` safe range to `[0.10, 0.15]s`, (c) preserve PM Rules 4 + 7 `current_lane` source-lane semantic during SLIPPING. `OnSlipMidpoint` is NO LONGER required by Pull-Wave; if PM has no other consumer, the delegate may be removed (PM's discretion). `/propagate-design-change` will be run at Phase 5 to formalize the propagation.
- **Wave Spawner** ↔ Pull-Wave (forward contracts authored here, not yet a Wave Spawner GDD): (a) PEAK pool avg velocity ≥ 1.5× OPENER pool avg (R1 RC-D); (b) `wave_id` monotonic allocation (Rule 14 (b)); (c) `FPullWaveCurveSnapshot` produced at admission, asset reference never passed to `Construct()`; (d) `IWaveSpawnerCallback` implementation receives `OnCollisionUnregistered` + `OnWaveDespawned` per Rule 13. Each must be re-verified when Wave Spawner GDD is authored.
- **Telegraph, Collision, Audio Controller, Near-Miss Detection, Death Replay**: Not yet designed. Each GDD when authored must list Pull-Wave as an upstream dependency with the interface specified above.

**Open ADRs that block implementation (not GDD acceptance):**

- **OQ-7 ADR (RSM UE object type)** — Determines the tick-prerequisite mechanism for the chain `RSM → DPC → PM → Pull-Wave → Telegraph → Collision`. Shared with RSM + DPC; already open.
- **PM OQ-2 ADR (Forward Motion Architecture)** — Pull-Wave's F-TRAJ-FORWARD assumes Option C (moving-world local frame). If PM ADR resolves differently, F-TRAJ-FORWARD must be re-derived.

## Tuning Knobs

| Knob | Type | Default | Safe Range | Gameplay Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` | float (m) | `15.0` | `[10.0, 25.0]` | Distance from player plane to wave spawn plane. Lower values shorten wave lifetime (less reaction time in TRAVERSING; combined with `LeanDurationS`, wave from spawn to land = ~1.4–2.5s); higher values lengthen wave visible time on screen (more dramatic approach but lower spawn density possible). Cook-time: `>25.0` warns about object pool sizing (EC-WAVE-LIFETIME-OVERRUN). |
| `PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MIN_MS` | float (m/s) | `4.0` | `[2.0, 6.0]` | Lower bound on per-pattern authored `ForwardVelocityMs`. Wave Spawner cook-time validation rejects patterns below this. Lower values produce long-hang slow pulls (rewards attentive reads); risk of stale pacing at OPENER if too low. |
| `PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MAX_MS` | float (m/s) | `18.0` | `[12.0, 24.0]` | Upper bound on per-pattern authored `ForwardVelocityMs`. Higher values compress reaction window inside TRAVERSING (LEANING duration is independent — only TRAVERSING shrinks). Above 14 m/s, cook-time tooling warns about combination with `LeanDurationS < 0.70s` (approaches survivability floor; R11a-arithmetic 2026-06-18 — raised 0.65 → 0.70s per R10d FLOOR CD ruling; was stale 0.6s pre-R5 B1). |
| `WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S` | float (s) | `0.15` | `[0.075, 0.5]` (R5 B6 — floor raised from `0.05`) | Time held in LANDED state after collision check fires, before transition to DESPAWNING. Allows Telegraph System's landing-impact VFX to complete on-screen. Lower values risk truncated VFX; higher values delay slot reclamation (impacts Wave Spawner's deferred queue if cap is full). **Cross-knob floor (R5 B6)**: floor MUST stay strictly greater than `NEAR_MISS_FLASH_DURATION_S = 0.066s` so the cool-highlight edge flash completes before DESPAWNING (Pillar 5 readability). At the new floor 0.075s, the 9ms margin tolerates registry-side rounding of `NEAR_MISS_FLASH_DURATION_S` without re-tuning this floor. The previous floor of 0.05s sat below `NEAR_MISS_FLASH_DURATION_S` and would truncate the flash mid-animation at floor configuration. Any future raise of `NEAR_MISS_FLASH_DURATION_S` past 0.075s requires raising this floor in lockstep. |
| `WAVE_DESPAWN_DISTANCE_M` | float (m) | `3.0` | `[1.0, 6.0]` | Post-collision visual follow-through distance behind player plane before mesh hide. Pure aesthetic — affects how the wave "lands off-screen" feel. Beyond this distance, wave is hidden but actor may persist until pool return. |
| `LANE_WIDTH_M` | float (m) | `1.0` (provisional) | `[0.75, 1.5]` | World-space width of each lane. Provisional pending PM revision — PM will own this constant post-revision (`LANE_OFFSET_CM` reorganizes into `LANE_WIDTH_M × signed offset`). Affects `world_x` output range in F-TRAJ-LATERAL: at default (5 lanes), lane centers span 4.0m (lane 0 at −2.0m to lane 4 at +2.0m); total track visual width ≈ 5.0m including half-lane margins. Mobile thumb-target at 6-inch screen ≈ 14mm per lane at default — comfortably above the standard mobile-touch floor; raising to 1.25m yields ~17mm/lane (more permissive); the 5-lane choice (R1 RC-F) is what makes default 1.0m mobile-safe. |
| `CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE` | float | `0.005` | `[0.001, 0.01]` | Tolerance margin for `LateralOffsetCurve` endpoint validation: `|GetFloatValue(0.0) − 0.0|` and `|GetFloatValue(1.0) − 1.0|` must both be within this tolerance. Cook-time validation only; runtime never checks. Looser tolerance allows authoring slop; tighter forces exact endpoints. |
| `WAVE_OFFSCREEN_TOLERANCE_M` | float (m) | `0.1` | `[0.0, 0.2]` | Cook-time tolerance for `world_x` extending past track bounds. Allows edge-lane pulls to bleed one voxel-unit to visual edge without clipping hitbox off the declared track. |
| `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER0_DEG` | float (degrees) | `0.0` | **LOCKED** (not tunable) | **Locked constant** for tier-0 (straight-forward) waves. `0.0°` is structurally bound by R4 RC-R3-2: the angle channel encodes magnitude; tier-0's magnitude is zero by definition. Any nonzero tier-0 angle would mis-signal a lateral commit on a straight-forward wave (Pillar 2 violation). Listed in the knob table for symmetric reference only; no safe range. |
| `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER1_DEG` | float (degrees) | `17.0` | `[14.0, 20.0]` | **Feel knob.** Maximum lean angle at `lean_progress = 1.0` for a 1-lane shift. Lower bound: must be visually distinct from "no lean" (`LEAN_ANGLE_TIER0_DEG = 0.0°`). Upper bound: must be visually distinct from tier-2. Tuned at prototype against 6-inch mobile screen discrimination. Do NOT raise above tier-2 value − 3 (R7 B6 — MIN_GAP joint constraint). (R2 Cluster C CD adjudication.) **Cross-knob invariants**: ordering + MIN_GAP per pattern 7a-7h. |
| `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER2_DEG` | float (degrees) | `22.0` | `[18.0, 24.0]` | **Feel knob.** Maximum lean angle for a 2-lane shift. Must be visually distinct from tier-1 and tier-3. Per-knob range overlaps with TIER1 and TIER3 ranges; joint binding constraint is the MIN_GAP=3 static_assert (R7 B6 — pattern 7e/7f) which catches any joint config with gap < 3° at build time. (R2 Cluster C CD adjudication.) **Cross-knob invariants**: ordering + MIN_GAP per pattern 7a-7h. |
| `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER3_DEG` | float (degrees) | `25.0` | `[21.0, 25.0]` (R7 B6 — upper tightened 27→25 so TIER3_max + MIN_GAP=3 = TIER4_min=28, closing the art-director R6-flagged adversarial gap-collapse path) | **Feel knob.** Maximum lean angle for a 3-lane shift. Must be visually distinct from tier-2 and tier-4. The 25°→28° gap (3°) is the narrowest inter-tier step and equals MIN_GAP floor at default — prototype must validate this is reliably perceived at mobile viewing distance before finalizing (forwarded to Telegraph GDD per AC-PW-22c). Upper bound tightened R7 B6 from 27 to 25 to prevent the adversarial TIER3=27 + TIER4=28 = 1° gap art-director flagged at R6 (both individually within their then-safe ranges; ordering 27<28 holds; gap collapses to 1° sub-perceptible). The MIN_GAP static_assert (pattern 7g) is the long-stop for TIER2/TIER3 pair. (R2 Cluster C CD adjudication; R7 B6 — defense-in-depth for MIN_GAP invariant.) **Cross-knob invariants**: ordering + MIN_GAP per pattern 7a-7h. |
| `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER4_DEG` | float (degrees) | `28.0` | `[28.0, 32.0]` (R7 B6 — lower tightened 25→28 so TIER3_max + MIN_GAP ≤ TIER4_min, paired with TIER3 upper tightening above) | **Feel knob.** Maximum lean angle for a 4-lane shift (maximum possible lane delta on a 5-lane track). Upper bound: physical plausibility (beyond ~35° the voxel mass reads as "falling over" not "leaning toward"). Lower bound tightened R7 B6 from 25 to 28 — at TIER3_max=25 (new R7 cap), TIER4_min=28 yields gap = 3 = MIN_GAP floor ✓. This is the angular ceiling of the entire lean-readability system. Tune as a pair with `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER3_DEG`. (R2 Cluster C CD adjudication; R7 B6 — defense-in-depth for MIN_GAP invariant.) **Cross-knob invariants**: ordering + MIN_GAP per pattern 7a-7h. |

| Constant | Value | Justification |
|---|---|---|
| `NUM_LANES` | `5` | Locked by Rule 2 (R1 CD adjudication 2026-06-07; supersedes R1-draft 6). At separation=1 yields C(5,3)=10 distinct M=3 configs (≥4 required, 2.5× margin). Changing this requires re-verifying AC against then-current `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION`, plus Player Movement revision, plus mobile thumb-target check. Raising above 5 reopens the mobile-UX check; lowering below 5 risks AC failure. Either direction requires CD sign-off. |
| `PLAYER_PLANE_Z` | `0.0` | Convention. In moving-world frame (PM OQ-2 Option C), the player actor is fixed at local Z = 0; track and hazards translate. Not a tuning concern. |
| `MIN_BARRAGE_LANE_SEPARATION` | `1` (FINAL R7) | Pull-Wave-owned, closed by F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT derivation. Was provisional under DPC; this GDD finalizes the value at 1. **Semantic locked (R2 Cluster E CD adjudication 2026-06-08): separation is measured on TARGET-LANE indices, not source-lane indices.** At target-lane distinct + value=1: adjacency permitted; C(5,3)=10 valid M=3 configurations pre-AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER (post-R7-B8 + R9 coord: 7 surviving M=3 triplets — see AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K). This semantic resolves the OQ-PW-5 convergence authoring question (source-lane reading was incorrect — Wave Spawner must enforce target-lane separation at pattern admission). Status: registry entry updates `provisional → active` at Phase 5. Raising requires re-verifying AC-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K AND re-deriving `MIN_ESCAPE_SLIPS` AND raising `NUM_LANES` to preserve ≥4 spatial configs. |

Worst-case escape slips across the 7 surviving M=3 PEAK triplets × 5 player lanes after AC-PW-PEAK-NO-ADJACENT-CLUSTER exclusion. **Decoupled from MAX_PULLS_PER_BARRAGE = 3**: barrage size remains 3; worst-case-escape distance is bounded by which triplets are admissible at cook time, not by M directly. Minimum patterns per admissible K_class in PEAK pool — closes the K_class-floor-at-ceiling vacuous-satisfaction gap (K_class=4 = K_class_max=4 admits singletons per class). Class-3 (singleton class with sole triplet `{0,2,4}`) satisfies the floor via the R8 RC-C B11 relaxation exception (mixed-tier barrages on class-3 admit ≥2 distinct configurations). Classes 4, 5, 6 satisfy naturally (each contains ≥2 raw triplets). See AC-PW-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K extended sub-checks (iii)+(iv). |

Tightening from 5→3 ensures tier-0's zero-rotation boundary case lands before the rotation-as-commitment model crystallizes. See AC-PW-OPENER-TIER0-INTRO sub-check (b). |

See AC-PW-OPENER-TIER0-INTRO sub-check (c). |

Prototype-validated perceptibility threshold at 6-inch mobile non-foveal viewing for rotational deltas on a 3×3×3-unit voxel mass. Below 3°, adjacent-tier waves read as visually identical (Pillar 2 violation — player cannot read magnitude correctly). Enforced at build time via AC-PW-22b patterns 7e-7h `static_assert` on `(TIER_i+1 - TIER_i) ≥ LEAN_ANGLE_MIN_TIER_GAP_DEG` for i ∈ {0,1,2,3}. Defaults (17, 22, 25, 28) sit at gaps 5/3/3 — last two pairs at MIN_GAP floor. Lowering this constant requires re-running the 6-inch mobile non-foveal perceptibility analysis with new prototype evidence; raising it forces tier-range collapse (e.g., raising MIN_GAP to 4° would force TIER3/TIER4 default 25→28 gap to widen). Registered to entities.yaml at Phase 5. |

Drives F-BARRAGE-SURVIVABILITY-INVARIANT as an additive term alongside `SLIP_TWEEN × M`. **Safe range: [0.20, 0.25]s.** Lower bound is the empirical mobile recognition floor — reducing below 0.20s requires platform-specific playtest evidence that SLIPSTORM's pre-telegraphed pattern structure meaningfully reduces acquisition latency, AND CD sign-off on the Player Fantasy survivability promise. **Knob interactions:**

- **`SPAWN_PLANE_Z_OFFSET_M` × `PULL_WAVE_VELOCITY_MAX_MS`** together set the minimum TRAVERSING duration. At default offset 15.0m × max velocity 18.0 m/s = 0.83s TRAVERSING. Total wave on-screen time at fastest tuning: 0.70s lean + 0.83s traverse = 1.53s (R11a-arithmetic 2026-06-18 — raised 0.65 → 0.70s per R10d FLOOR CD ruling; matches F-TRAVERSE-DURATION line 393 worked example; was stale 1.48s under pre-R10d FLOOR=0.65s, pre-R5 B1 stale 0.6s). Tune as a pair if pacing feels too rushed at PEAK.
- **`LANE_WIDTH_M` × `NUM_LANES`** together set center-to-center track span and total visual width. At default (1.0m × 5 lanes): center span = 4.0m, total visual width ≈ 5.0m including half-lane margins — fits comfortably on a 6-inch portrait mobile screen at typical viewing distance. Raising `LANE_WIDTH_M` to 1.25m yields 5.0m center span / 6.25m total — still within mobile budgets but starts to push the upper bound. Raising `NUM_LANES` above 5 widens center span and re-opens AC-PILLAR-2-BARRAGE-SPATIAL-K verification AND the mobile thumb-target check.

### Implementation Resource Budgets (R1 RC-G fixes)

These are not designer-facing tuning knobs — they are implementation-side budget formulas surfaced here so an implementer can size pools and material parameter banks correctly. Changing the defaults requires CD + technical-director sign-off.

**Object pool sizing (R1 RC-G G-1; R8 perf-analyst B-1 — formula re-derived against WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S safe-range ceiling 0.5s; `+5` magic constant replaced with explicit MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP_CEIL_MARGIN; R10c — Pull-Wave R9 performance-analyst F1 2026-06-17 — middle term inert under cap-includes-LANDED semantics; formula refactored to drop the inert term and introduce explicit `DESPAWNING_RETURN_LATENCY_SLOTS` for above-cap pool-return-deferral).** Pull-Wave's per-instance pool MUST size to:
```
pool_size ≥ MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP
         + DESPAWNING_RETURN_LATENCY_SLOTS
         + MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP_CEIL_MARGIN

at default tuning (post-R10c):
  pool_size ≥ 16 + 2 + 5 = 23
```
where `DESPAWNING_RETURN_LATENCY_SLOTS = 2` covers the above-cap DESPAWNING-state instances awaiting pool-return (Rule 13 step 5-6 MAY defer one frame; under MAX_SPAWN_RATE_PER_S ≈ 1.0/s the expected per-tick despawn count is ≪1 at steady state, but two slots cover the worst-case overlap of a deferred ReturnToPool with a fresh natural-landing or a stale ReturnToPool tail from a prior tick — derivation in R10c amendment below), and `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP_CEIL_MARGIN = 5` is the explicit safety-margin constant (R8 perf-analyst B-1 — replaced the prior anonymous `+5` magic constant). The `+5` margin covers (a) lifetime overrun at extreme tuning per EC-WAVE-LIFETIME-OVERRUN AND (c) growth headroom for prototype iteration without re-allocating the pool. **R10c amendment — margin rationale (b) re-homed**: the R1/R8 margin rationale item (b) "one tick of DESPAWNING + pool-return latency" is now carried by the explicit `DESPAWNING_RETURN_LATENCY_SLOTS = 2` term and is STRUCK from the margin's (a)+(b)+(c) breakdown to prevent double-counting; margin = (a)+(c) only post-R10c. **R10c — Pull-Wave R9 performance-analyst F1 finding**: the R8 derivation `pool_size ≥ MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP + ceil(MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP × WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S_CEIL × MAX_SPAWN_RATE_PER_S) + MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP_CEIL_MARGIN = 16 + 8 + 5 = 29` over-derived the pool size: the middle term `ceil(cap × hold × rate)` was intended to model LANDED-hold wave overlap above the cap, but Rule 12's `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP` enforcement already counts LANDED waves as active (cap is enforced on LEANING + TRAVERSING + LANDED active states; only DESPAWNING is above-cap per Rule 13's "atomic per wave despawn pipeline" — step (iii) `Despawned` fires AFTER cap decrement). Under cap-includes-LANDED semantics, the middle term is INERT — it double-counts LANDED-hold which is already absorbed by `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP`. The correct above-cap accounting is the DESPAWNING-state pool-return-deferral, which is bounded by `DESPAWNING_RETURN_LATENCY_SLOTS = 2` (one slot for the expected per-tick despawn at MAX_SPAWN_RATE_PER_S=1.0/s, plus one slot for the deferred-frame overlap when Rule 13 step 5-6 defers ReturnToPool to the next tick). **Binding default post-R10c: pool_size = 23** (R9 performance-analyst F1 — corrected from R8 B-1's 29; the WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S safe-range ceiling 0.5s no longer enters the formula because LANDED-hold is in-cap by Rule 12, not above-cap). Confirm at prototype against measured mobile spawn-rate envelope; if `MAX_SPAWN_RATE_PER_S` exceeds 1.0/s at PEAK in measurement OR if pause-flush bursts produce sustained above-cap DESPAWNING-deferral exceeding 2 slots in profiling, re-derive `DESPAWNING_RETURN_LATENCY_SLOTS` with measured value. **R10c traceability note**: the prior R8-binding `WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S_CEIL = 0.5s` value is preserved in §Tuning Knobs as a per-wave LANDED-hold tuning bound for designer-error detection; it no longer participates in pool sizing post-R10c.

**Per-instance material parameter routing (R1 RC-G G-2 — ISM batching invariant; R7 B9 — trail rendering separated to its own ISMC because trail cubes are NOT instances of the wave-mass mesh).** Wave-mass material scalars (`LeanChargeIntensity`, `NearMissEdgeFlash`, `VoxelDissolveFade` — 3 scalars on the wave-mass ISMC) MUST be routed through **`PerInstanceCustomData`** on the wave-mass Instanced Static Mesh Component (HISM or ISMC), NOT through per-instance Material Instance Dynamic. The forbidden pattern (`MaterialInstanceDynamic` per wave) forces one draw call per instance — at MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP = 16 simultaneous waves + cluster-internal voxel sub-meshes, this would explode to ≈128–224 draw calls on mobile, exceeding the typical mobile tile-based GPU draw budget for an in-game frame. The required pattern (`PerInstanceCustomData[N]` indexed on the wave-mass ISMC: 0=LeanCharge, 1=NearMissFlash, 2=VoxelDissolve) keeps ALL wave-mass instances in a SINGLE batched draw call regardless of count. Material shader reads via `GetPerInstanceCustomData(N)`. **R7 B9 — Trail rendering ISMC separation (NEW; performance-analyst R6 RECOMMENDED escalated; closes architectural contradiction that "single batched draw call" included the trail cubes)**: the prior R1 RC-G G-2 spec listed `TrailAlpha` at `PerInstanceCustomData[3]` on the wave-mass ISMC as if the 3 trail-ghost cube meshes (Traverse motion / TRAVERSING section) were instances of the wave-mass mesh — they are NOT. Trail cubes are axis-aligned 1-unit cubes; the wave mass is a 8–14 voxel irregular cluster within a 3×3×3 bounding box. A separate **trail-cube ISMC** (`TrailCubeISMC`) is required, sized for `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP × 3 = 48 trail-cube instances` at PEAK. Trail-cube ISMC routing: `PerInstanceCustomData[0] = TrailAlpha` (cutout threshold per-instance fade scalar — see TRAVERSING motion section). At PEAK density, total batched draw call count for Pull-Wave visuals is **2 ISMC draw calls** (wave-mass ISMC + trail-cube ISMC), NOT 1 — the prior "single batched draw call" claim is corrected to "single batched draw call per ISMC; 2 ISMCs total." Material shader reads via `GetPerInstanceCustomData(0)` on the trail-cube material instance. Pool sizing for `TrailCubeISMC`: 48 instances (16 active waves × 3 trail cubes); slot allocation is wave-bound — when a wave despawns, its 3 trail-cube slots return to the trail-cube ISMC free list. **R7 B9 ADR-class question (queued for technical-director sign-off)**: whether to expose trail-cube ISMC as a separate component in the Pull-Wave actor or hoist to a project-level shared ISMC. This is an Implementation-Resource-Budgets concern; queued at OQ-PW-3 (mobile thermal validation at prototype). This is a binding implementation rule — not optional.

**Trail rendering technique (R1 RC-G G-3 — mobile tile-GPU budget).** The motion trail (Section F-VISUAL TRAVERSING motion) MUST be rendered as **alpha-tested (cutout) voxel-ghost meshes**, NOT as translucent ribbon quads. Rationale: mobile tile-based GPUs incur disproportionate bandwidth and thermal cost on translucent surfaces (full pixel re-read per layer); at 16 simultaneous waves × ~3-4 trailing quads each = ≤64 translucent overdraw layers per frame in the wave region. Alpha-tested cutout produces hard edges with zero overdraw cost — it composites in opaque pass. **Design-intent shift flagged**: the original R1-draft spec (translucent fade-from-40%-alpha ribbon) implied a soft "ghost motion" read; cutout produces a more graphic, comic-book-style ghost. This is a deliberate aesthetic shift toward Pillar 4 (Surreal in Motion — sharp-edged voxel consistency) AND toward mobile feasibility. **Art-director sign-off REQUIRED at prototype** to confirm the cutout ghost reads as "approaching motion" not "static after-image." OQ-PW-7 tracks this validation gate.
- **`WAVE_DESPAWN_HOLD_S` × `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP`** together set the slot reclamation pipeline pressure. Long hold + high cap = deferred-queue backpressure if Wave Spawner is cap-bound.

**Designer-error classes** (legal at runtime but ship-blocking):

- **Out-of-bounds curve authoring** (EC-CURVE-OVERSHOOT): `LateralOffsetCurve.GetFloatValue(t) > 1.0` at any t. Wave swings off-screen. Detectable by cook-time tooling; not a runtime fault.
- **Endpoint drift** (EC-CURVE-ENDPOINT-DRIFT): curve endpoints outside `1.0 ± CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE`. Wave doesn't land at exact target lane. Cook-time tooling rejects.
- **Velocity out of range** (EC-ZERO-OR-NEGATIVE-VELOCITY): `ForwardVelocityMs ≤ 0`. Cook-time tooling rejects.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Art bible not yet authored. This section works from game-concept pillars and voxel/surreal/kinetic style anchors. Art bible authoring is a required step before asset production begins (see OQ-PW-6).*

### Wave Visual Identity (voxel mass at rest and at spawn)

**Shape.** Each pull-wave is an irregular blocky cluster of 8–14 axis-aligned unit voxels arranged in a roughly forward-leaning wedge: dense at the core (5–7 tightly packed voxels), sparser at the trailing edge (3–5 offset cubes spaced ½ unit apart). The silhouette reads as a single heavy mass from behind but breaks into individual cube faces up close. No smooth edges, no rounded corners. The cluster's bounding box fits within a 3×3×3 unit cube. "Iron debris" intent: identifiable individual blocks, not an amorphous blob and not a perfect geometric solid.

**Size relative to avatar — hitbox vs. visual silhouette (R1 RC-B fix).** The avatar is defined as a 1-unit "soft thing" (game-concept). Two distinct extents apply:

- **Visual silhouette (perceived mass).** The wave cluster's DENSE CORE (the 5–7 tightly packed central voxels) MUST fit within **1.0 × LANE_WIDTH_M wide** (≤1.0m at default tuning) — this is the visual extent that drives "which lane is this in" reads. The SPARSER TRAILING edge voxels (3–5 offset cubes) MAY extend up to **0.25 × LANE_WIDTH_M into each adjacent lane** (≤0.25m at default), producing a perceived overall silhouette width up to ~1.5 × LANE_WIDTH_M. This bleed is acknowledged and acceptable: the offset cubes read as "iron debris in motion," not as "this wave is in two lanes."
- **Collision hitbox (gameplay extent).** The wave's collision volume — the box Collision GDD evaluates at LANDED — is EXACTLY `LANE_WIDTH_M × LANE_WIDTH_M × cluster_depth` centered on `target_lane`. Hitbox NEVER bleeds into adjacent lanes regardless of silhouette extent. Pillar 5 binding: a player in an adjacent lane is genuinely safe from a wave targeting the next lane over even if the visual silhouette overlaps. The avatar's hitbox is similarly lane-anchored (PM Rule 2).

Cluster height: 1.5 avatar-units tall (unchanged). The wave must visually dominate its lane core at the spawn plane on a 6-inch mobile screen; the trailing-edge bleed reinforces "heavy mass" perception without compromising Pillar 5's gameplay-safe-lane semantic.

**Color and material.** Base palette: dark warm iron gray (RGB approx. 55–65, 50–60, 45–55 — provisional; art bible will lock exact values). Specular highlight band along the top faces only, driven by a flat toon-style two-tone material: lit face at ~30% brightness over base, shadow face at base only. No gradients. The color must read as distinct against both the track surface and sky background at 6-inch screen distance — flag for art bible to specify track/sky contrast requirements explicitly.

**SPAWNED state (one tick, hidden).** The wave mesh is fully transparent (`opacity = 0.0`) during the SPAWNED tick. No visible presence. Fade-in begins at LEANING entry, not at SPAWNED — a hard pop into visibility at lean-start is acceptable and preferred over a slow spawn fade that could confuse the telegraph timing.

**Idle motion.** During LEANING (forward-stationary), the mass does NOT vibrate or idle-animate before the lean begins. Static at lean_progress = 0.0 is the ground state. Motion begins only when lean_progress begins advancing. Rationale: any pre-lean idle motion would degrade Pillar 2 by reducing contrast between "at rest" and "telling you where it is going."

### Lean Visual (LEANING state — primary telegraph contract)

**Tilt axis and angle range.** The mass rotates around its base-contact lateral axis (X-axis in local space, perpendicular to the forward travel direction). Rotation is in the direction of `target_lane` relative to `source_lane`. Rotation angle maps `lean_progress` and `lean_magnitude_tier` as follows:

```
lean_angle_deg = lean_progress × LEAN_ANGLE_DEG_BY_TIER[lean_magnitude_tier]
```

where `lean_magnitude_tier` is pre-computed at SPAWNED entry (see Data Contract sub-section below) and `LEAN_ANGLE_DEG_BY_TIER` maps each tier to a distinct angular ceiling (see Magnitude Encoding sub-section). At `lean_progress = 0.0` the mass is vertical. At `lean_progress = 1.0` the mass leans to the tier's full angle toward `target_lane`. No deformation, no skew — rigid rotation only. The voxel cluster stays in block form throughout.

**Animation curve (R7 B7 — promoted to registry-backed canonical asset with cook-time fingerprint check; supersedes "design intent" parenthetical; R10b extended 2026-06-16 — interpolation mode pinned).** `lean_progress` drives the rotation with an ease-in-out curve sampled from a **registry-canonical `UCurveFloat` asset** named `LeanEaseCurve_Canonical` (registered in `design/registry/entities.yaml` with `source: design/gdd/pull-wave-behavior.md`; asset path `Content/Game/PullWave/Curves/LeanEaseCurve_Canonical.uasset`). Control points: `0.0→0.0, 0.4→0.15, 0.7→0.7, 1.0→1.0` — these are NOT design intent guidance, they are the binding registry-locked values. **Interpolation mode (R10b — pinned 2026-06-16 per R9 systems-designer F1; was implicit in fingerprint targets, now binding metadata)**: every `FRichCurveKey` on the canonical asset MUST have `InterpMode = RCIM_Cubic` AND `TangentMode = RCTM_Auto` (the UE UCurveFloat editor default — the pin makes it explicit and cook-time-verifiable). Linear interpolation contradicts the fingerprint target at t=0.25 (linear between `(0.0, 0.0)` and `(0.4, 0.15)` yields 0.094 at t=0.25; the fingerprint demands ~0.038 — only reachable under cubic Hermite with auto tangents). The pin matters for the EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY arithmetic magnitude: cubic delivers `t_perceptible_direction ≈ 0.13–0.15s` (the value the survivability invariant assumes); linear would shift it by ~30ms and break the invariant arithmetic. **Cook-time fingerprint check (R7 B7 — NEW; extended R10b 2026-06-16 — interpolation-metadata check added)**: Wave Spawner authoring tooling validates the canonical asset at admission via **two checks**: (1) **Control-point sample check** — sample at five reference t values (0.0, 0.25, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0) and assert each return value within `CURVE_ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE = 0.005` of the registry-locked target (0.0, ~0.038, 0.15, 0.7, 1.0). (2) **Interpolation-metadata check (R10b — NEW)** — iterate the asset's `FRichCurve` key array and assert each key's `InterpMode == RCIM_Cubic` AND `TangentMode == RCTM_Auto`; any other combination halts admission with an error citing R9 systems-designer F1 + EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY. The two-check fingerprint is the long-stop against a substituted standard cubic ease-in-out preset (which returns ~0.10 at t=0.4 — check (1) catches it) OR a linear-interpolated variant on the same control points (which returns 0.094 at t=0.25 — check (1) ALSO catches it; check (2) catches it pre-evaluation as defense-in-depth and as a clearer error message for the authoring tooling user). AC-PW-22b NEW pattern 12 (R7 B7) FORBIDs inlined `UCurveFloat*` member literals in the rotation-driver source path; the canonical curve MUST be loaded by registry name. Rationale: ease-in-out ensures the lean "charges" with the most visually readable motion happening in the middle of the window, not front-loaded (which would leave a long static period) or back-loaded (which would compress the readable moment near TRAVERSING entry). Registry-locked control points + pinned cubic interpolation ensure brightness phase (driven by the same curve per Ease-curve binding) stays aligned with rotation phase (Pillar 2 windup-before-punch ordering) AND that the perceptibility-onset arithmetic in EC-EASE-CURVE-ONSET-DELAY remains the load-bearing reference figure for survivability math.

**Magnitude encoding across lane distances (5-tier including tier-0 — R2 Cluster C CD adjudication 2026-06-07; R4 RC-R3-2 tier-0 first-class addition).** Lane distance does NOT scale the angle proportionally. Each distinct lane-shift magnitude (including zero) maps to a distinct angular ceiling, giving the player five visually separable tiers (max lane delta = 4 at 5-lane track; min = 0 for straight-forward waves):

| `lean_magnitude_tier` | Lane-shift magnitude `abs(target_lane − source_lane)` | `LEAN_ANGLE_DEG_BY_TIER` | Degrees at `lean_progress = 1.0` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0-lane shift (straight-forward; `target_lane == source_lane`) | `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER0_DEG` | **0°** (locked; no rotation — angle channel carries magnitude, magnitude is zero) |
| 1 | 1-lane shift | `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER1_DEG` | **17°** |
| 2 | 2-lane shift | `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER2_DEG` | **22°** |
| 3 | 3-lane shift | `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER3_DEG` | **25°** |
| 4 | 4-lane shift | `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER4_DEG` | **28°** |

**Tier-0 first-class status (R4 RC-R3-2).** Straight-forward waves are valid patterns (Wave Spawner authoring permits `SourceLane == TargetLane`). Tier-0 is NOT special-cased as "no telegraph" — it is a telegraph that says "the wave is coming on lane X, on schedule." Zero rotation on the angle channel encodes destination identity (same lane). The brightness channel continues to ramp `1.0× → 1.8×` per the same eased curve used at tiers 1–4 (R4 user decision; see "Color and material shift during lean" below). Rationale: per R3 CD adjudication #2, angle carries magnitude and brightness carries phase; these are independent channels. A tier-0 wave with no rotation but a ramping brightness signature reads as "approaching on this lane, no lateral commit" — Pillar 2 contract satisfied in the time dimension even when spatial information is zero. **A tier-0 wave is NOT visually mute during LEANING** — that interpretation would degrade the read-and-react loop by making "no lean" indistinguishable from "no wave yet."

**Cross-knob ordering invariant (R3 editorial sweep; R5 B3 — safe-range truthfulness clarified; R7 B6 — MIN_TIER_GAP defense-in-depth added).** The five tier angle constants MUST satisfy `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER0_DEG = 0.0` AND `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER1_DEG < LEAN_ANGLE_TIER2_DEG < LEAN_ANGLE_TIER3_DEG < LEAN_ANGLE_TIER4_DEG` at any tuning configuration. **The per-knob safe ranges in the Tuning Knobs section are INDIVIDUAL knob bounds, not jointly-safe** for all tier pairs. TIER1=[14,20] ∩ TIER2=[18,24] = [18,20] (overlap; joint constraint via MIN_GAP static_assert); TIER2=[18,24] ∩ TIER3=[21,25] = [21,24] (overlap reduced R7 B6 — TIER3 upper tightened 27→25; joint constraint via MIN_GAP static_assert); **TIER3=[21,25] ∩ TIER4=[28,32] = ∅** (non-overlapping R7 B6 — TIER3 upper tightened 27→25, TIER4 lower tightened 25→28; per-knob safe ranges now structurally enforce MIN_GAP for the highest-consequence tier3/tier4 pair without relying on the joint constraint). **Cross-knob MIN-GAP invariant (R7 B6 — NEW; covers all pairs as defense-in-depth)**: `LEAN_ANGLE_MIN_TIER_GAP_DEG = 3.0°` MUST hold between every adjacent tier pair — i.e., `(TIER1 - TIER0) ≥ 3.0 AND (TIER2 - TIER1) ≥ 3.0 AND (TIER3 - TIER2) ≥ 3.0 AND (TIER4 - TIER3) ≥ 3.0` at any tuning configuration. Rationale: 3° is the prototype-validated minimum perceptibility threshold at 6-inch mobile non-foveal viewing for rotational deltas on a 3×3×3-unit voxel mass. Below 3°, adjacent-tier waves read as visually identical — a Pillar 2 failure (a tier-3 vs tier-4 confusion on a 5-lane track means the player at lane 0 cannot tell whether the safe escape is right-3-lanes or right-4-lanes, a 50/50 lottery on the highest-consequence read). The narrowest defaulted configuration (17, 22, 25, 28) has gaps 5, 3, 3 — the last two pairs sit at MIN_GAP floor. **Enforcement**: AC-PW-22b pattern 7 (sub-patterns 7a-7d) enforces strict ordering; **R7 B6 new sub-patterns 7e-7h** enforce MIN_GAP — both required for green CI. The TIER3/TIER4 pair has two layers of defense: non-overlapping per-knob safe ranges (structural) AND the MIN_GAP static_assert (joint). For TIER1/TIER2 and TIER2/TIER3 pairs, per-knob safe ranges still overlap (preserving tuner flexibility) and the MIN_GAP static_assert is the sole joint guard — adversarial picks like TIER1=20 + TIER2=22 = gap 2 fail the static_assert at build time. Tuners changing any tier angle MUST re-verify the joint MIN_GAP, not just the individual knob's safe range. The Pull-Wave authoring tool SHOULD surface a warning when joint configurations approach the MIN_GAP floor; cook-time validation is the long-stop.

**Rationale (Pillar 2 binding).** Pillar 2 ("The World Telegraphs Before It Strikes") requires that the player read *destination* from lean alone — before any slip is committed. On a 5-lane track, the two longest shifts (3-lane and 4-lane) have critically different escape implications: a player at lane 0 seeing a 3-lane right-lean has a safe lane adjacent to the target (lane 4 is safe); a player at lane 0 seeing a 4-lane right-lean has no safe lane to the right and must go left. Under the prior 3-tier spec, both mapped to 28° — a 50/50 lottery with zero pre-slip information, which directly broke the Player Fantasy promise ("its mass commits to a destination lane… lands where the lean said it would land"). The 4-tier spec closes that disambiguation gap. The 3°–5° inter-tier steps are intentionally compressed at the high end (22→25→28°) to bias visual range toward the 1-lane vs 2-lane discrimination that drives the most frequent barrage escapes; the existence of four distinct angles is the contract, not their equal spacing.

**Perceptibility note (prototype validation required).** The 25° → 28° gap (3°) is the narrowest inter-tier step. At 6-inch mobile viewing distance on a voxel mass of declared 3×3×3-unit bounding box, 3° rotational delta is at the edge of reliable perceptibility. Telegraph System authoring of the angle-to-visual mapping (including any ease-curve or camera-FOV compensation) MUST validate that tier-3 and tier-4 are reliably discriminated at prototype before lean-magnitude readability is signed off. See Telegraph GDD forward contract below.

**Telegraph forward contract (binding when Telegraph GDD is authored; R4 RC-R3-2 + RC-R3-4 extensions).** Pull-Wave exposes `lean_magnitude_tier` (0–4) to Telegraph System per tick during LEANING. Telegraph GDD MUST:
1. Consume the 5-tier mapping above as its authoritative angular source — no independent angle constants. **Tier-0 is first-class** — Telegraph MUST render a tier-0 wave with zero rotation AND the standard brightness ramp; it is NOT a silent/hidden state.
2. Include an AC asserting that for each `lean_magnitude_tier ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3, 4}`, the rendered rotation at `lean_progress = 1.0` equals `LEAN_ANGLE_DEG_BY_TIER[tier] ± 0.1°` (tier-0 asserts exact `0.0°`).
3. Validate in prototype that tier-3 (25°) and tier-4 (28°) are reliably discriminated at 6-inch mobile screen distance; adjust visual compensation (ease curve weighting, FOV, voxel silhouette contrast) if needed before this discrimination is signed off.
4. **Provide register/unregister observability** (R4 RC-R3-4): expose a Telegraph-side hook OR participate in Seam 13's extended `OnTelegraphUnregistered(WaveId)` callback (see "Despawn pipeline" Rule 13 + Seam 13 R4 extension) so AC-PW-15's step (ii) is independently observable from the test stub, not inferred from downstream side effects.

This forward contract echoes the R2 CD adjudication binding. Pull-Wave owns the tier definitions and the behavioral state; Telegraph owns the visual rendering. Neither may independently change angle values without the other's corresponding update.

**Data contract exposed to Telegraph System.** Pull-Wave exposes the following fields for the Telegraph System to consume per tick during LEANING (read via poll or `OnLeanProgress` subscription — Telegraph GDD decides the interface):

- `lean_progress` — `float [0.0, 1.0]`
- `source_lane` — `int32 [0, 4]`
- `target_lane` — `int32 [0, 4]`
- `lean_direction_sign` — `int32 {-1, 0, +1}` (sign of `target_lane - source_lane`; 0 = straight-forward wave)
- `lean_magnitude_tier` — `int32 {0, 1, 2, 3, 4}` (0 = straight-forward / 0-lane shift; 1 = 1-lane; 2 = 2-lane; 3 = 3-lane; 4 = 4-lane; pre-computed at SPAWNED entry as `abs(target_lane - source_lane)` and used to index `LEAN_ANGLE_DEG_BY_TIER` in the 5-tier Magnitude Encoding sub-section above — R2 Cluster C CD adjudication; R4 RC-R3-2 promotes tier-0 to first-class with `LEAN_ANGLE_TIER0_DEG = 0.0`. Tier-0 waves rotate by 0° throughout LEANING but DO ramp `LeanChargeIntensity` per the standard eased curve — see "Color and material shift during lean" below.)

Telegraph System is responsible for all rendering from these fields. Pull-Wave does not drive rotation transforms directly.

**Color and material shift during lean (R4 RC-R3-6 — `LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO` promoted to registry; R6 B3 — safe-range floor tightened against dark base material).** As `lean_progress` advances from 0.0 to 1.0, the wave's specular highlight band intensity ramps from the base value to `LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO × base` (registry constant — see `design/registry/entities.yaml`; current value `1.8`; safe range `[1.7, 2.2]` — R6 B3 raised floor from `1.5` because at the dark iron-gray base palette RGB 55–65 / 50–60 / 45–55 a 1.5× peak yields only ~28-point RGB lift on the lit face, below reliable non-foveal perception threshold at 6-inch mobile distance. The 1.7× floor delivers ~42-point lift, comfortably above non-foveal threshold). **Material-dependency note**: the safe-range floor is conditional on the base-material luminance lock by the art bible. The 1.7× floor applies when the base palette stays in the current RGB ~60 average band. If art-bible-lock raises base luminance to RGB ≥100 (e.g., a brighter "iron" reading), the floor may be re-evaluated downward — re-derive against the new palette and re-validate at 6-inch mobile non-foveal perception. Conversely, if art-bible darkens the base further below RGB 60, the floor must rise above 1.7×. This is a uniform top-face brightness scale, not a hue shift. At `lean_progress = 1.0`, the entire mass is at peak brightness before it releases into TRAVERSING. The effect reads as the iron "charging" before it moves — surreal but controlled. Implemented as a `LeanChargeIntensity` material scalar routed through **`PerInstanceCustomData[0]`** on the wave-mass ISMC (per R1 RC-G G-2 binding — see Implementation Resource Budgets).

**Channel-separation rationale (R3 CD adjudication #2 — documented per R4 RC-R3-2).** The brightness ramp is uniform across ALL five `lean_magnitude_tier` values — including tier-0 (straight-forward) and tier-4 (4-lane shift). It does NOT scale with magnitude. The rationale is explicit dual-channel encoding:
- **Angle channel** carries MAGNITUDE (which destination, how far): tier 0 = 0°, tier 4 = 28°.
- **Brightness channel** carries PHASE (when does it commit; how close to release): linear ramp 1.0× → 1.8× regardless of tier.

Driving brightness from magnitude would create a conflict-resolve dilution — a tier-0 wave that holds at base brightness would read as "no wave is coming" until TRAVERSING, defeating the lead-time Pillar 2 requires. A tier-4 wave that flashes brighter than tier-1 would create double-coding (player has to read both intensity AND angle to determine destination). The two channels are kept orthogonal: angle answers "where," brightness answers "when." Tier-0's brightness ramp is therefore NOT a special case — it is the design's core "no wave is mute during LEANING" invariant, applied at the boundary.

**Ease-curve binding (R2 Cluster F — Pillar 2 ordering fix; R4 RC-R3-2 — tier-0 included).** `LeanChargeIntensity` MUST be driven by `EaseCurve(lean_progress)` — the same `UCurveFloat` asset that drives the rotation (control points `0.0→0.0, 0.4→0.15, 0.7→0.7, 1.0→1.0` per the Animation Curve section above) — NOT by raw `lean_progress`. Formula (identical for all five tiers, including tier-0):

```
LeanChargeIntensity_scalar = 1.0 + EaseCurve(lean_progress) × (LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO − 1.0)
```

where `LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO` is the registry constant (current value `1.8`; safe range `[1.7, 2.2]` — R6 B3; lower floor 1.5 retired against dark iron-gray base — see "Color and material shift during lean" above for material-dependency note). **Output range** (R8 sys-d R-3 — declared): `LeanChargeIntensity_scalar ∈ [1.0, LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO]` at `lean_progress ∈ [0.0, 1.0]`; at default `LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO = 1.8`, output ∈ [1.0, 1.8]; at safe-range ceiling 2.2, output ∈ [1.0, 2.2]; output is a multiplicative scalar applied to the base specular highlight, so the on-screen lit-face RGB rises proportionally (e.g., base RGB 60 × 1.8 = RGB 108 on lit face at peak). Worked phase check: at `lean_progress = 0.4`, `EaseCurve(0.4) ≈ 0.15`, so `LeanChargeIntensity ≈ 1.12×base` — matching the rotation's 4.2° / 28° = 15% phase at tier-4 (or 2.55°/17° at tier-1; or 0°/0° at tier-0 — rotation is zero throughout, but the phase reference is preserved). At `lean_progress = 1.0`, `LeanChargeIntensity = LEAN_BRIGHTNESS_PEAK_RATIO × base`. This binding ensures brightness and rotation are always in phase (Pillar 2 ordering: windup is visible before punch; brightness does not lead the rotation signal). Driving brightness from raw `lean_progress` (linear) caused brightness to reach +32% at `lean_progress = 0.4` while rotation was still at 4.2° — a punch-before-windup inversion. Telegraph System writes `LeanChargeIntensity_scalar` to `PerInstanceCustomData[0]` each tick during LEANING using this formula. Brightness peak is uniform across all five `lean_magnitude_tier` values including tier-0 (R2 Cluster C + R4 RC-R3-2); brightness scales with progress (phase), not with magnitude (see Channel-separation rationale above). No emissive glow, no color temperature shift — brightness only (Pillar 2: readable against track and sky on small screen).

### Traverse, Landing, and Despawn Visuals

**TRAVERSING motion (R7 B9 — trail ISMC architecture clarified).** The mass translates rigidly along its trajectory (no per-frame rotation during transit). A directional motion trail is rendered as **alpha-tested (cutout) voxel-ghost meshes** behind the mass (NOT translucent — R1 RC-G G-3 binding; see Implementation Resource Budgets). The trail comprises **3 axis-aligned cube meshes** (1-unit cubes — NOT instances of the wave-mass irregular cluster mesh) offset behind the mass at half-unit intervals, each shrinking 30%/55%/80% along the trajectory tail (the rearmost ghost is the smallest). Each ghost cube is rendered with a trail-cube material modulated by a per-instance alpha-test threshold (`TrailAlpha` scalar via `PerInstanceCustomData[0]` on the trail-cube ISMC — R7 B9) that produces a hard-edged "ghost fade" — sharp voxel silhouettes, no soft falloff. **Architecture (R7 B9 — trail-cube ISMC separate from wave-mass ISMC)**: the 3 trail cubes per wave are instances on a dedicated `TrailCubeISMC` component sized for `MAX_CONCURRENT_WAVES_CAP × 3 = 48 trail-cube instances` at PEAK. The wave-mass ISMC and trail-cube ISMC together produce **2 batched draw calls** at PEAK (1 per ISMC), NOT 1 — the R1 RC-G G-2 "single batched draw call" claim is corrected to "1 batched draw call per ISMC; 2 ISMCs total for Pull-Wave visuals." The trail communicates forward velocity and direction at a glance; it is a choreographed VFX element, not a physics particle system. No debris, no sparks, no chaotic secondary emitters. **Aesthetic note**: R1-draft specified translucent quad ribbon with soft alpha fade; the cutout-ghost approach is a deliberate Pillar 4 (sharp voxel consistency) + mobile-tile-GPU shift. Art-director sign-off required at prototype (see OQ-PW-7).

**Approach intensity (REMOVED — R4 RC-R3-6; R3 CD adjudication #1 binding).** The R3-prior "final-25% ghost-trail scale-up to appear to accelerate without altering the simulation" cue is **REMOVED**. Rationale: Pillar 5 ("Skill Is Visible — no mystery deaths") forbids perceptual deception. A player who learns to time dodges to a visual acceleration cue is reading a false signal — the simulation never accelerates (Rule 6: spawn-time velocity binding, no mid-flight update). If experienced players learned this cue they would mistime late-flight dodges relative to the simulation truth. The cue would teach an incorrect mental model.

Forward velocity remains constant per Rule 6.
